mr president, commissioner, in this debate i have heard arguments which are very similar, if not identical, to those used in the united states of america by the arms industry to combat the prohibition of the possession and use of weapons. i would like to say that the safety of citizens does not come from the possession or use of firearms, it comes from the right public safety policies. the security of citizens depends on the police, depends on government policy measures, not on citizens carrying weapons. the gun, in the possession of citizens, undermines collective security. the only weapon that should be allowed to the human being is the word, is the argument, are the ideas. the possession and use of firearms, commissioner, mr president, are the remnants of barbarism, situations which should not be allowed in a civilized society. hunting and the use of weapons, even in hunting, are part of a culture of death and not of a culture of defence of life, especially of civilized life in modern societies.
the commission has also proposed that the council adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. mr president, commissioner, as regards the recycling targets for packaging and packaging waste, given the wide disparity in the starting points of the member states in relation to these targets, now revised upwards, which determines the need for different efforts to achieve the targets set, without compromising the objectives, some temporal flexibility in their implementation is justified, taking into account the diversity of situations. it was a concern of the opinion adopted by the committee on industry. on the other hand, the existence of new, upwardly revised objectives must justify the existence of new means of achieving those objectives, and this is an aspect which has been neglected so far. extended producer responsibility schemes are appropriate both for the prevention of packaging waste and for the establishment of systems ensuring the recovery and/or collection of used packaging and packaging waste from the consumer or the waste stream, along with the reuse or recovery termino , including the recycling of packaging and packaging waste collected, in accordance with the waste management hierarchy.
mr president, in any viable scenario for the future of the european union, the circular economy must be at the centre of attention. the circular economy is an example of the kind of measures we need to put in place to make citizens feel the positive impact of our work on their lives. i welcome the excellent work done by the rapporteur, simona bonafè, and by the whole team who worked on this legislative package. the shadow rapporteurs proposed that the transition and energy efficiency be promoted, that landfilling be phased out until it is completely eliminated, that landfilling of waste that is separately collected be prohibited, that a framework programme be set up for a timetable of targets that further encourage and facilitate separate collection, sorting and recycling, that the transfer of waste within the european union and to third countries be monitored, that an impact study be carried out on the advisability of setting a target for the total amount of waste that can be landfilled, calculated per kilogram, per person and per year. the circular economy has to be more than a good narrative, commissioner. it must be a concrete and positive reality and we have the conditions now to do so.
mr president, we are discussing today the circular economy package, which goes beyond the reductive approach to waste management and recycling, marking a time of redesign of processes, products and new business models. the linear model paradigm no longer responds to the contemporary challenges of resource availability, environmental and social problems arising from the globalisation of markets, the conservation of natural capital, the reduction of emissions and the fight against climate change. the landfill directive will clarify the waste hierarchy, which determines a priority order from prevention through preparation, to reuse, recycling and energy recovery to final disposal. landfilling, although the worst option in the waste hierarchy, is still the most widely used waste disposal method in the european union. we have to change the way we collect and manage our waste. the transformation of waste into resources is crucial to increase resource efficiency and close the loop in a circular economy. this landfill directive is precisely the answer to this ambition.
mr president, a tragedy occurred two days ago in ethiopia with more than 60 people, mostly women and children, losing their lives in a landslide at a rubbish dump. this happened in a country which is a major recipient of funds from the eu. as well as expressing our solidarity with the people of ethiopia, we should ask the commission to enquire into the level of misgovernance and oppression that the eu is funding in ethiopia today, where people are losing their lives at the mercy of a dictatorship which is totally anti-democratic.
madam president, how will the european union act consistently in the un human rights council in an era when the civilizational progress achieved over decades in international human rights law is being threatened by some of its main promoters, as is the case with the election of president trump in the united states, or as is the case with the european union itself, which is entering into an immoral deal with turkey on refugees and which leaves unpunished member states such as hungary that attack, detain and expel refugees and curb the free press and human rights organisations? i would like to emphasize, however, the courageous work of high commissioner al hussein in denouncing serious human rights violations throughout the world, including in authoritarian and repressive regimes that are protected by the united states and the european union itself. i give as examples the massacres of irreecha, ethiopia, in 2016, and the massacre of mount sumi, angola, in 2015, for which the un high commissioner called for independent investigations. the european union has an interest and an obligation to defend the functioning, integrity and independence of the office of the high commissioner for human rights and to enhance the work of the un human rights council. this is all the more crucial when president trump threatens to cut 50 percent of the united states' contribution to the un, striking at several agencies and calling into question the multilateral system, which, despite all its inadequacies and deficiencies, has kept humanity from precipitating a collective catastrophe.
madam president, the un human rights bodies are now called upon to deal with setbacks in this field, all over the world. in addition to the situations which have been rightly identified as priorities in the resolution tabled by my group, i would like to use this speech to refer specifically to the situation in latin america. the human rights situation in several latin american countries is very worrying. this region has too high a rate of human rights violations. take, for example, the real epidemic of violence linked to criminal organizations and corrupt security forces that is affecting central american countries such as el salvador, honduras, guatemala, and mexico today. in addition to the thousands of deaths and the climate of widespread impunity, this violence is creating hundreds of thousands of internally displaced persons in what is already a genuine migration crisis. i cannot fail to mention venezuela, where at the moment there are hundreds of political prisoners in humiliating conditions. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force. many of the human rights violations in this region have a particular historical and cultural context. this is the case for indigenous peoples, whose rights to their ancestral territories continue to be disrespected, in parallel with the criminalization of environmental activism and the persecution of those who defend the rights of these minorities. it is true that some progress has been made, but reforms that are truly capable of protecting human rights in a consistent way are lagging behind in most latin american countries. it is time for the un to pay more attention to the human rights situation in this important region of the globe.
mr president, this long work on tax fraud and tax evasion reflects a very concrete reality, and if it were not for the multiple tax scandals that have brought this issue to public attention, it would still be in the shadows of the european union's government offices. we now know that tax fraud and tax evasion are widespread throughout the european union and that governments continue to make tax deals with multinationals while punishing their own people with austerity policies. in other words, tax fraud and tax evasion are not the result of spontaneous generation, nor do they represent any dysfunction of the system. tax fraud and tax evasion are one of the increasingly visible features of the capitalist system and the subordination of political power to economic power. only a political break with this system, by giving way to public control of the financial system and capital movements, can pave the way to a lasting solution that can effectively combat this phenomenon and put an end to the tax evasion of multinationals sponsored by the european union and its member states.

the speech of compassion and compassion in relation to the famine in somalia, nigeria, yemen and south sudan cannot erase the need to look at the root causes of this humanitarian tragedy, nor the proper allocation of responsibilities. the reasons for the famine are political in the first place. the interventions of the imf and the world bank and the prevailing neoliberal orientations, the significant increase in the cost of the factors of production in agriculture, inseparable from the power and role of the multinationals, the drastic reduction in the purchasing power of the urban population, the dismantling of social programs, the debt and debt service garrote, the replacement of the state grain reserves and the mechanisms for fixing prices of agricultural products on local markets by the chicago stock exchange, by a world market dominated by the multinational agribusiness and speculators, the civil wars and the various conflicts instigated by external powers such as the united states, saudi arabia and european countries, the external interference and the imperialist drive to control natural resources such as oil. all of this has led to what the united nations has called the greatest tragedy, the greatest humanitarian crisis, since its inception in 1945. a change of policy is needed and emergency humanitarian aid is needed, with the european union taking greater responsibility. at a time when scientific and technical development is reaching levels that would easily eradicate hunger, its persistence is one of the most vivid libels against the world-dominant capitalist system.
madam president, we are witnessing a digital revolution, driven by the acceleration of technology, in the capacity to capture, process and transmit data. this revolution is an opportunity to reshape the society we live in, making it more people-centred, less unequal and more sustainable. so this revolution cannot be driven by technology. it is technology that must be instrumental in relation to the vision of the european gigabit society that we choose to realize. i welcome the draft legislative resolution under consideration and, in particular, the excellent work carried out by my colleague, mrs patrícia toia. taking on board the virtualities of the commission proposal, the agreement ensures improvements in the good allocation of spectrum resources in the european union, allowing the development of a new generation of technology-leading and inclusive wireless broadband services. this is also the philosophy behind the wifi4eu initiative, of which i am rapporteur, and which aims precisely to provide european citizens with a good experience of access to the european gigabit society, regardless of their economic situation or geographical location. in summary, madam president, the project under consideration is a very important step towards the realisation of a digital union, which will put the european union back on the front line of a revolution in the service of people, the economy and the quality of life of europeans.
mr president, beyond scenarios the european union needs a clear choice. the clear choice should be to do much better together on the relevant priorities. therefore, i believe that the real debate to be developed is on whether – and how – we should deliver on these relevant priorities, first of all providing our citizens with better working and living conditions. all working people in europe should count on a clear contract with fundamental rights and full access to social protection. this is a central piece of the social pillar which must be included in the rome declaration. we also need to make sure that more investment and better jobs will be delivered with stronger means. that is why the rome declaration must include a full reform of the eurozone with the banking union and the fiscal capacity. we need to make sure that europe remains a central pillar of the multilateral system and for international cooperation, but we also need to make sure that the next trade agreements will enforce our social and environmental standards. most of all, the rome declaration needs to assert, 60 years later, the european union as a powerful, political, economic, social and cultural entity, but most of all a democratic entity. that is why we need to fully involve european citizens and the european parliament in defining this rome declaration. the crisis of the process of european capitalist integration is inseparable, on the one hand, from the more general crisis of capitalism and, on the other, from the matrix characteristics and objectives of the process itself. sixty years after the signing of the treaty of rome, the treaty establishing the european economic community (eec) and the european atomic energy community, the eec/eu is facing the most serious crisis of its existence. the depth and persistence of this crisis mean that for the first time it is openly admitted that integration may lead to disintegration, which will be manifested by the united kingdom's withdrawal from the european union, known as brexit. contrary to what some people are trying to make us believe, this crisis is not the result of short-term factors. the deep roots of the crisis lie in the (capitalist) nature of the integration process. any leaps forward without changing this matrix will only deepen the crisis. what europe and its peoples are demanding is not more of the same, but a project of breaking with this eu, aimed at building another model of development, a europe of the peoples of cooperation and development that respects the sovereignty of each state.

mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner king, a year ago, the barbaric situation was in brussels. before it had been paris and copenhagen and then it would become nice, munich and berlin. nothing justifies terrorism and what matters is to prevent it, because no sanction against criminals reduces the suffering of the victims. i repeat what i have said many times here: we have always linked the freedom of movement with the safety of our citizens. these are two fundamental rights which we have a duty to protect. in the last year we have reacted in a hot-button fashion, with member states making advances and retreats, but we have undoubtedly made some important steps. we have strengthened europol, guaranteed the rights of victims, increased controls at external borders and tackled radicalisation. however, as roberta metsola said, we can do more and better. i have always advocated the best use of the instruments we have on the ground, rather than the multiplication of announcements of new measures. the schengen information system is a prime example. the system most used by the police remains with its potential yet to be fulfilled. but the review of its legal basis will soon make better information available to the competent authorities. that's the message i want to highlight here today. in the fight against terrorism, mutual trust, greater cooperation and better information are essential. that should be our focus on the work ahead.

member of the commission. – madam president, this is an important question. the commission is a strong supporter of a vibrant, dynamic system of political parties active in a european-level public space. this is important in our european union based on democracy, as we have just heard. it contributes to the democratic foundations of our work together, including some of the bases for the election of the commission president and the appointment of the commission. we therefore welcome the opportunity to discuss the question of european political parties and foundations today, in particular – as has just been noted – the regulation adopted by parliament and the council in november 2014 is now coming into effect. the new regulation brings into play important innovations. these include a european-level legal personality for parties and foundations, and a more robust set of governance arrangements, including a new authority for european political parties and foundations, and enhanced safeguards to ensure respect for fundamental european values by all parties registered under the scheme. on this last point, it is now a condition for registration as a european party or foundation that the entity concerned respects, both in its programme and in its activities, the values on which the european union is founded: respect for human dignity, freedom, democracy, equality, the rule of law, and respect for human rights, including the right of people belonging to minorities. this clarity is most welcome. furthermore, at any time after registration, parliament, the council or the commission may ask the new authority to verify whether a particular party or foundation continues to comply with these requirements. after consulting a new permanent committee of independent eminent persons, the authority can decide to deregister a party or foundation which is found not to comply, unless parliament or the council disagrees. this is an important standing safeguard. so the new regulation is a welcome and substantive improvement to the rules governing political parties and a good defence instrument for our european values. beyond this, the commission has taken good note of the suggestions for further possible changes to the regulation, which are outlined in the oral question in front of us today (the initiative of the committee on constitutional affairs). we take these suggestions very seriously. even good rules can be further improved. the commission is fully prepared to assist parliament with well-reflected initiatives and proposals. of course, in line with our better regulation principles, our normal practice – as you know – is to evaluate existing legislation before proposing changes, and to assess fully the potential impact of such changes. as president tajani always says, quality is often as important as speed. i am pleased that the regulation includes a very early review clause with first parliament and then the commission being obliged to report on its implementation in the coming months. the commission’s report will be accompanied by proposals for any legislative changes that appear necessary. the commission is more than willing to engage, in depth, with parliament and the council to monitor closely the early stages of implementing the new regulation and to reflect together on whether early proposals for change might be justified. the commission will be very open, if you agree, to setting up quickly an interinstitutional process – perhaps under the auspices of the new authority – to review developments, assess suggestions from stakeholders, and hopefully converge on a common approach. this would also help pave the way for the formal reviews that must in any case take place next year. turning to the specific suggestions, i would like to share some initial comments while stressing again that the commission rules nothing out at this stage. first, on reducing the co-financing requirement. in 2012, the commission, taking up a request from parliament, proposed just such a change. unfortunately, this met with strong opposition in the council and was not included in the final text adopted by the co-legislators. as things stand, it is not clear that a new proposal on these lines would meet a different fate. second, on increasing the level of representation required in this house before applying for eu funding. we need to consider carefully the principles of proportionality and equal treatment, since the rules on party funding should not tilt the balance in favour of incumbents, compared with new political movements, provided of course these new movements also uphold european values. third, on cross-membership. the issue here is whether meps from a national party which is affiliated to a european party should be able to be members, on an individual basis, of different european parties. the new regulation already makes clear that in such circumstances, the individual membership does not count towards the representation threshold for funding of a european party. could a similar restriction apply to the threshold for registration? we can think about it and examine the potential consequences. on the other hand, we are not aware of an objective basis for limiting choices of meps in this regard when their national party is not affiliated to a european one. fourth, is the question of whether european parties should be able to finance campaigns for national referendums on eu issues. the commission would like to hear the views of parliament and member states on this important but sensitive matter. as concerns several more technical issues such as a financial capacity criterion, we are happy to take a closer look. parliament could also see how far its own implementing rules could be adjusted to help within the framework of the existing regulation. finally, we see many arguments against allowing reserve-building by parties and foundations. this would seem to go against the budgetary principles of annuality and sound financial management, and would increase risks to the control of eu contributions by making it more difficult to identify and correct possible irregularities. it would make it more likely that, should there be irregular spending, it would have to be recovered after european elections rather than before, which could prove sensitive for reasons we can all imagine. so at this stage, we continue to think the existing n+2 rule strikes the right balance. as i said, the commission is keen to engage on all these issues with parliament and with the council, so we look forward to hearing all of your views in today’s debate.
on behalf of the alde group. madam president, commissioner, public funding of european political parties is of the utmost importance for the rule of law, but it generates conflicting tensions within the democratic system. on the one hand, public subsidies to parties reduce the need for their private funding, and consequently the propensity for corruption, i.e. for the proliferation of public decisions in exchange for private payments or advantages. but on the other hand, public funding of parties generates a huge capacity to turn political struggle into a professional activity, which becomes attractive for its remuneration and personal benefits rather than for its intrinsic purposes of public service and citizenship. the most harmful consequence of the professionalisation of political activity is that the professional interests of political actors overlap or predominate over the collective interests of the citizens they represent. moreover, this professionalisation favours cloudiness and opacity rather than transfer. transparency not only in the allocation of funding but also in the way political parties spend that money. the best response to preventing these dangers lies more in creating mechanisms that effectively ensure such transparency, as well as effective public scrutiny of party spending, than in yielding to populist pressures or impulses to reduce or make it more difficult for european democratic parties to access public funding. the money of european taxpayers will only be well spent if it is used only to achieve the public ends that have determined the collection of taxes from citizens. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 149 (8))
answer according to the "blue card" procedure. i am an unfailing defender of freedom of expression and have been for more than fifty years, since i have known myself as a citizen, first against the fascist dictatorship in portugal and then against other attempts to limit it. in this respect, mr coburn, i adopt the maxim of montesquieu, or rather voltaire, that although i do not agree with anything you say, i have fought with all my strength so that you may say it freely. freedom of expression is the twin brother of accountability and abuses of freedom of expression in the political struggle must be fought politically and not administratively or through the courts.
in writing. this is a report in which we make actionable recommendations in the light of the current eu legal framework for effective cooperation in the area of security and defence. we believe that the european defence agency (eda) and the permanent enhanced cooperation mechanism (pesco) should be treated as eu institutions sui generis and financed from the union budget, which needs to be considerably strengthened. we stress the role that the eda should play in coordinating between member states, in particular in capacity building and equipment acquisition, with economic and efficiency gains. we call for the establishment of a permanent seat for the csdp and the pesco, and we clarify that no member state wishing to join the pesco should be excluded. we call for the reform of the athena mechanism to ensure a fair distribution of operational costs and for member states to be encouraged to contribute forces, not being restricted by their financial capacities. more than ever, the eu needs to equip itself with the tools to ensure its own security and defence, in the face of threats common to all member states and with the growing unpredictability of acting within nato, clearly breached by trump, whatever his ministers say.
madam president, the biodiversity of the arctic and the nature of its ecosystems make it critically important for the biological, chemical and physical balance of the entire planet. industrial resource exploitation therefore poses special challenges in the arctic and calls for urgent international cooperation. the destruction of the boreal ice is already releasing methane from permafrost soils, thus amplifying global warming. going any further is like fueling a fire with gasoline. the arctic should be seen as our canary in a coal mine, as my colleague mrs schaldemose said, an alarm system to speed up action to reduce the human processes that amplify global warming. exploring arctic resources has to be viewed with great concern. a new source of fossil resource exploitation will not be compatible with the ambitions of a decarbonised economy and the paris agreement. the european union is in a privileged position to facilitate cooperation and dialogue between the countries concerned. three principles should emerge: protection, sustainable and precautionary use and international cooperation. the fact is that today's economies are living in a state of toxic dependence on fossil fuels, a civilization alcoholicism that we don't want to get out of. opening the "cabaret of the last chance" in the arctic, where we will drink more and more, and one more glass, will be the ultimate sign of civilization's recklessness.
madam president, the times we live in require clear positions from all global actors and the european union in particular in the most sensitive areas for the sustainability of the planet. the signals given by the new us administration and the possible contagion of other global resource exploiters is a great risk for the planet, but at the same time it opens a window of opportunity for european leadership in the transition to the green and blue economy. i therefore stress the importance of this own-initiative report. the environmental conditions of the north seas are so sensitive and vulnerable that even a slight disturbance in their structure can have irreversible consequences. the arctic region is already suffering the impacts of climate change, with glacier melt accelerating. the increase in the exploitation of oil and gas reserves in the region would increase the imbalance of its ecosystems. in the context of the energy transition in all areas of society, and in particular in transport, a cautious approach such as that proposed in the report under consideration seems to me to be the most appropriate.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the social, environmental and economic challenges facing the arctic region are great: a small population spread over a large area, the dramatic effect of climate change, increasing competition for access to its natural and mineral resources and the opening up of new shipping routes and fishing areas. the arctic is also a region of growing geostrategic importance. the european union is not a party to the treaty of rome, but it is a party to the treaty of rome, and it is not a party to the treaty of rome. scientific assessments show us clearly that this is a fragile and endangered ecosystem, that it has more than half of the world's wetlands, that its summer sea ice volume has decreased by more than 40 percent in 35 years, and that the temperature increase has been increasing at a rate approximately twice the global average. ladies and gentlemen, climate change in the arctic is having a major impact on coastal regions worldwide, including in the european union. coastal erosion and rising sea levels are just some of the environmental problems caused by climate change in the arctic, which are being felt drastically, in some cases, around the world. i therefore consider this own-initiative report to be an important step towards the european union taking a leading role in safeguarding the environmental fragility of the arctic and the fundamental rights of indigenous peoples and local populations. but, ladies and gentlemen, time is running out and it is time for action, not mere intentions.
madam president, the operating models of democratic societies are being radically reconfigured by the adoption of new information and communication technologies. this report is therefore very timely. i welcome the rapporteur, ramón jáuregui, for the concise, pragmatic and structured way in which he identifies the potential and the challenges of e-democracy. the acceleration of technology must not call into question the fundamental values of representative democracy. on the contrary, technology should be used to strengthen it. i would like to highlight three dimensions, in the time i can, that are fundamental: the qualification of citizens for the digital society, transparency and equity in access to relevant information and, finally, the possibility of spreading the possibility of face-to-face electronic voting. these are three opportunities and three lines of work for us to harness technology to strengthen democracy. thank you very much for the excellent report.
on behalf of the s&d group, i would also like to thank the rapporteur, our colleague marco affronte, as well as the other shadow rapporteurs and the representatives of the institutions at trilogue level. i think we have a good regulation. as the socialists and democrats' shadow rapporteur for this report, i would stress that scientific data on fisheries have been essential for knowledge-based management and decision-making. the revision we are approving here opens up new possibilities for cooperation between member states, making cooperation at regional level more coherent. as i myself come from a scientific institution dealing with fisheries data, i stress the importance of the consistency of the information collected. this new regulation also emphasises the possibility of scientific cruises and not just the acquisition of biological data from the landings of commercial fisheries. scientific cruises, independent of commercial fishing, are key to completing a holistic view of resources. another very important aspect is greater transparency in access to data resulting from the programmes covered by the regulation. institutions with a valid reason may request the fisheries data obtained from the collection systems financed at european level. this diploma will promote transparency and will certainly result in better knowledge to support better management. however, there is concern about the lack of guarantees for the participation of regional authorities in the planning and collection phases. as the bodies most closely dealing with the problems, in many cases regional governments do have responsibilities in managing resources and may, if this is the member state's interpretation, be left out of the process. i'll keep an eye out and make sure no one gets left out. finally, i would like to stress the elegance with which the question of the legitimacy of monitoring and implementation of the programme was resolved. after a lively and intensive legal dialogue at trilogue level, an effective solution was reached. the mixed approach between delegated and implementing acts legitimises the specificity of each of the european governance institutions.
on behalf of the alde group. madam president, i would also like to start by congratulating the rapporteur on his excellent work and express my solidarity with the approach he has put forward in his report, which is to stress the need for scientific data to be able to assess the impacts of fishing, not only in terms of the reproductive capacity of stocks, in terms of quantity, but also and above all in terms of the environmental sustainability of this economic activity, which is so necessary for economic development and so necessary for the livelihood of millions of families around the world. i would like to say that sustainability must necessarily include the environmental issue. there is no sustainability if we don't protect the environment and if we don't all get involved in this project and this work to protect the environmental quality of the oceans. only then will fisheries be sustainable, only then will fisheries be able to fulfil their economic objective. but on the other hand, i would also like to stress that a balance must be struck between the interests of professional fishing and the interests of tourism associated with recreational fishing in certain regions. there are situations where recreational fishermen have privileges over professional fishermen and this is not acceptable. finally, madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the policy of collecting and consolidating data for the purpose of good management of fisheries policy is only possible if there is investment, increased investment in scientific research. more money is needed for scientific research in the oceans, which will enable reliable data to be collected, which can fulfil its informative mission.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster is a good one. the need to collect, manage and use fisheries data in sufficient quantity and quality is indisputable, as is the need to make these data comparable and integrable at national, regional and international level. there is a dimension to resource management that obviously goes beyond national borders, and this requires genuine cooperation and concerted action at international level. what is questionable, however, is whether these needs are better met within the framework of the centralized vision that continues to prevail in the european union's common fisheries policy. this vision is concretized in the institutional framework provided for in the treaty of lisbon, which confers exclusive powers on the european union in this field, something which has always been in our disagreement. time has shown the pernicious nature of this view. moreover, the european union, which wrongly has exclusive competence in policy-making, is shifting a substantial part of the costs of financing that policy onto the member states, failing to assume the full responsibility for financing, for example, the acquisition and management of data. do we want more and better data? i'm not sure. we want greater scrutiny and greater transparency in their use, but we do not support a view of resource management which we believe is fundamentally wrong.

madam president, commissioner, palm oil is widely used in the food industry and also in the cosmetics and biofuels sectors, and the truth is that over the last 20 years its production has become unsustainable and has resulted in massive deforestation, the loss of natural habitats, the near extinction of many species and the consequent increase in greenhouse gas emissions. many cases of child labor and the expropriation of indigenous lands for palm oil production are also reported. in this context, the european union - the third largest market for this raw material in the world - has a major responsibility. it should take a leading role in combating wasteful and abusive consumption of palm oil and adopt minimum sustainability criteria for its certified palm oil imports, and also phase out the use of these vegetable oils in biofuel production, increase traceability and labelling, and inform consumers. this report is a step in the right direction and i congratulate the rapporteurs on that.
madam president, last week in the town of mocoa in southern colombia, 250 people lost their lives as a result of heavy rains and landslides that devastated 17 neighbourhoods in that town. in the capital of indonesia, a similar incident took place last week that claimed the lives of several people and left many others missing. around the world, these kinds of calamities occur, usually affecting those who are the most vulnerable in society: the poor and the destitute. the cause is recurrent: the intensive destruction of forest habitats through constant deforestation for agricultural exploitation and for the construction of infrastructure such as dams and levees. i believe that now is the right time for the european union to promote an action plan against deforestation and forest degradation, as well as against palm oil production. there are many vast problems today affecting tropical ecosystems, which occupy only 7 percent of the earth's surface. palm oil is increasingly being used as biofuel and a substantial part of its production is not respecting human rights, using child labour. the report stresses that respect for fundamental human rights, forest protection and sustainable palm oil production are compatible objectives. the european union must now strive to ensure that by 2020 all palm oil consumed in europe is sustainable. i hope that tomorrow's vote will reflect the urgency of action.
madam president, commissioner, this oral question is of undeniable importance, since such important matters as food safety and the protection of public health are at stake. the entry into the european union of spoiled meat, from whatever source, cannot be tolerated. the community authorities are responsible for taking the necessary steps to ascertain what has happened and to remedy it immediately. what we cannot do is use this particular case as a pretext to call into question the success of the negotiations under way to establish a trade association agreement between the european union and mercosur. it is worth recalling that this case was detected and denounced by the brazilian authorities themselves and affects only a small part of the vast meat production sector in that country. it refers to 22 companies, of which only 4 exported to the european union. the european commission has already stated that the current situation of brazilian meat does not represent an obstacle to the negotiations on the eu-mercosur agreement. we have worked with the brazilian authorities to solve this plant health problem. we must not be tempted to turn this into a serious problem of trade relations between two regional blocs which have every interest in ensuring their political, economic, cultural and commercial rapprochement.
a few words as president of the delegation for relations with brazil. firstly, to emphasize that the words to be conveyed to our fellow citizens must be of enlightenment, reassurance and confidence. the authorities on both sides of the atlantic have acted quickly and, as far as we know, effectively. however, we have a market under pressure and if we are talking about brazilian meat today, in the past we have already talked about mad cow disease, poorly labelled equine meat, bird flu, and even the warnings from the world health organization. and in fact, when the meat is on the plate, few consumers know its origin or its nationality. we are facing a problem which affects everyone and not just the meat producers in a particular country. companies in this value chain with sales in europe are therefore required to strictly comply with the legislation, but the competent authorities are also required to ensure compliance. secondly, to reaffirm that we are dealing with a court case. i therefore underline the merit of the investigation by the brazilian authorities, which identified these irregular practices, and the speed, transparency and effectiveness of the subsequent actions. in conclusion, i would like to remind you that the mercosur-eu trade agreement is welcome, because, in addition to the obvious economic benefits, it will strengthen the closeness and cooperation between our peoples.
madam president, european farmers have made great efforts over the years to produce better, to the highest standards in the world, the highest standards in terms of animal welfare, food safety and, in the case of beef production, individual traceability, as required by european legislation. it is obviously important that europe, when importing animals from third countries, should at least require the same requirements from exporting countries as it does from european farmers, so as not to destroy the confidence of consumers in the meat sold in europe, which has cost european farmers so much effort and the european union so many economic resources. in this case we must recognize that it was the brazilian authorities themselves who denounced the case, which means that the control in brazil worked, but at the same time it means that in the european union the control was not effective, because if the meat was adulterated it was not detected as such in the european union and it is this internal control in the european union, madam president, that we must also question.
in trade agreements i have been advocating a regulated market which guarantees food safety for products entering the european union and fair conditions for their production. we must protect european farmers from unfair competition by not allowing products to enter without the same production criteria as those we require in the european union, and by ensuring fairness in costs. we cannot accept two criteria, one more permissive for outsiders and one more demanding for our farmers. the situation in question is criminal and one-off, but it has revealed serious control failures which are systemic. adulterated and adulterated meat is being introduced into the union to the detriment of our farmers because of a lack of fairness. i am in favour of mercosur, but i am in favour of a 'regulated' mercosur and that is why i am now advocating a review of the mercosur negotiations on meat, on the grounds of consumer protection and the protection of european farmers.
madam president, the brazilian judicial authorities have identified various frauds in some of the meat consignments exported from brazil to europe and other parts of the world. this is unfortunate! but it should be emphasized the effectiveness of the operation that detected the fraud and the quick reaction of the authorities who suspended companies, launched audits and prosecuted the state officials involved. another dimension of the scandal is the impact it could have on food security in brazil, or beyond. we have to protect the consumer. this investigation has to go all the way. but in this context, it is not acceptable to use this case as a weapon to undermine the trade agreement between the european union and mercosur. in the current world economic and political order, to let a strategic rapprochement between europe and a large part of latin america slip away because of sectoral calculations, would also be a great scandal, a weak view of an operation called 'weak meat'.
the brazilian federal police recently uncovered a massive fraud scheme involving the sale of adulterated meat involving the country's leading brands. 29 companies were investigated, including the two giant jbs, friboi, seara and big frango brands, and brf, owner of sadia and perdigão. the list of irregularities investigated included the repackaging of products at the end of their shelf life, the injection of water to increase the weight of the goods and the use of ascorbic acid and sorbic acid to mask signs of meat deterioration. both acids are carcinogenic when used in high doses. the commission has also been informed that the commission has not yet adopted a new decision on the application of the union state aid rules to the aid scheme for the coal and steel sector. it is essential that the european commission takes stock of this scandal coming from what is today the world's largest exporter of meat and the largest supplier of beef to the european union and that it takes measures to protect consumers and to avoid any doubts about the quality of meat traded in the european area. it is also imperative that this issue has consequences for the current negotiations with mercosur.
madam president, commissioner, i would also like to congratulate the rapporteurs on their work. this regulation is important, but it does not meet one of the main ambitions of the european small-scale fisheries sector in terms of definitions. it does not define what is artisanal fishing. it seems to me that the role of the european union in this case will be to lay down general rules so that this definition can be established regionally. small-scale fishing is dependent on the region in which it is carried out. i know that relinquishing the power of standardisation is a difficult step for the european commission, but also for the council and, to some extent, for parliament, which dreams of harmonizing legal procedures at the level of an entire continent. but just as article 349 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union enshrines regional specificities, i suggest that the commission should propose legislation giving the regions of europe the capacity to define their artisanal and traditional fisheries.

madam president, i voted in favour of this report because i believe that it is an appropriate response to the defence of the internal market and consumer rights, allowing vehicles, including those already in circulation, to meet safety and environmental requirements. the unprecedented scandal over the manipulation of car emissions tests has revealed flaws in the approval system for new cars. we can't fool ourselves. this has undermined citizens' confidence in the european institutions and the automotive industry. after more than a year of intensive work, we have now reached the moment to discuss and vote on this important report so that this scandal does not happen again. the rationalization and strengthening of the competences on car emissions, greater european supervision of the competent national authorities, the creation of vehicle approval procedures and tests, the existence of tighter market controls and a strict exchange of information between the european union and the member states, and a system of dissuasive sanctions, will allow more effective results in the defence of consumer rights. the success of the implementation and enforcement of car emissions legislation depends on the active involvement of the european institutions, economic operators and, in particular, the automotive industry and citizens.

on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, i would also like to greet the presidency of the council, the commissioner and all my colleagues. the multiannual financial framework must respond to new challenges, needs, urgencies and unforeseen events. so, at a good time - and i pay tribute to all those who were involved in this negotiation - we in parliament fought through the last negotiations on the financial framework and achieved more flexibility in the budget of the european union and the possibility of having a revision of the multiannual financial framework. it was worth it, as the council has now recognised, albeit belatedly. this year, for the first time in history, we have the possibility to approve the revision of the multiannual financial framework 2014-2020. we have an obligation to increase employment, especially youth employment, to combat terrorism, to support those who need it most. to this end we have reinforced the initial amounts with some eur 5 billion - new money that was not foreseen. the youth employment initiative takes the biggest share with €1.2 billion. the research and innovation programme 'horizon 2020', the cosme programme for smes, the 'connecting europe' mechanism, erasmus+ are other programmes that have been strengthened, albeit with reallocation. we will thus have more resources to meet the challenges of the present and we will also increase the amounts earmarked for it to deal with the problems of migration. this revision introduces flexibility, makes it possible to finance additional needs, to strengthen employment and to promote solidarity. conclusion: it is a good review and all those who agree with these objectives should support it and the amounts are not for the european parliament, the amounts are for the european union, the institutions, the member states to face the challenges i have mentioned. it was also important to remove the constraints, the limitations, the overall commitment margin, to increase the overall payment margin, to increase the reserve for emergencies and a flexibility instrument. i am talking about special instruments and here i am turning to the council and saying that they should also have the right interpretation - and the right interpretation is that these special instruments, as the name implies, are special - and that means that they are above the limits, outside the limits of the multiannual financial framework. i am sorry that the council has once again tried to provoke and influence parliament. as is abundantly clear, a political declaration does not take away any of our prerogatives, much less those which are enshrined in the treaties and which are binding in terms of respect for our powers, the powers in the annual budgetary procedure, and we will maintain them. i would like to conclude, mr president, by thanking the commission for its bold proposal and in particular commissioner georgieva and the two presidencies for the success we have finally achieved, even though we are aware of the difficulties, the delays and the need for unanimity in this matter.
we are discussing today the revision of the multiannual financial framework. a short financial framework, less and less geared to cohesion and strengthening the fields of foreign policy, which we voted against at the time. the budgetary execution of the first half of the mandate has finally demonstrated the correctness of our position. the divergences between countries and regions are increasing - with peripheral countries trapped in austerity with low levels of investment and rising indebtedness - and the european union's own indebtedness is also increasing, bringing back the specter of cyclically recurring default. this revision, instead of addressing this reality by increasing cohesion funds, ends up by accentuating the problems we have denounced by mobilising, above all, the instruments of flexibility for the security and citizenship and foreign policy lines, to the detriment of measures that promote job creation. we are thus still dealing with a budget which fails to respond to the real problems of the european union by giving priority to the military and security aspects.
mr president, once again we are legislating against the backdrop of the financial crisis and the recognised role that the monetary funds have had and can continue to have in multiplying risk and losses, putting the whole financial system at risk. we all remember the closure of several monetary funds in recent decades. we all know that these funds represent one of the visible faces of the so-called 'shadow bank'. history has taught us that speculation is in the genome of finance. this proposal is intended to mislead the public and workers into thinking that it is possible to regulate this activity by creating new categories of funds, so-called low volatility funds, by imposing rules on diversification or even choices between investment portfolios. we are therefore faced with a complex and minimalist set of rules which do not address the real problems of the financial system and which once again demonstrate who is in charge in the european union.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the prospectus is a step towards building a so-called 'capital markets union'. underlying this proposal is this vision of our smes, financed through the capital market, promoting the public issue of equity securities to be traded on the stock exchange. this view, taken from the textbooks of financial economics, is unfortunately not in keeping with our economic and social reality. we do not think that micro-enterprises - the backbone of our economy - will be able to take advantage of this type of financing. regardless of the doubts that remain about this proposal in terms of exemptions, secondary emissions or the lack of sanctioning provisions to enforce compliance, we remain deeply disagree ing with the priorities of this european union. what companies need is not a single capital market. what they need are sound banks, preferably public ones, which are committed to development strategies and job creation and, for the time being, to a policy of raising wages which increases the purchasing power of households and actually gives confidence to businesses and the economy.
madam president, i would like to put forward our position and show it with three quotes from shakespeare, a great british and european writer. first, our position, our attitude as committed pro-europeans, is very well described in hamlet when horatio describes the expression of the father of hamlet: ‘a countenance more in sorrow than in anger’ – that will be our attitude in our negotiation. next, i would like to give a piece of advice to our british colleagues with another quote, because you should be very aware of what your bard shakespeare has said: ‘we know what we are, but know not what we may be’. finally, i would like to answer mr farage with a quote from shakespeare that is probably a fake quote, which is very, very appropriate to someone that is a post-truth politician: ‘love me or hate me, both are in my favour. if you love me, i'll always be in your heart. if you hate me, i'll always be in your mind’.
madam president, when many expected brexit to be the beginning of the end of the european project, the broad cross-party political consensus in support of the resolution we are going to adopt today on the brexit negotiations is in itself a very important sign of unity and hope. it is also important that parliament has set a clear priority to ensure that the rights of european citizens residing in the united kingdom and british citizens residing in the european union are respected on a reciprocal basis. and it is not respecting the rights of citizens, subjecting them, as is the case in the united kingdom today, to 85 pages of bureaucratic red tape in order to obtain their right to residence permits. the freedom of movement is fully binding on the united kingdom until the day of its withdrawal and even afterwards as long as a transitional regime for access to the single market is in force. this parliament will not give up on the defence of the rights of european citizens.
in writing. once again, the eu institutions are not compatible with democracy and the sovereign decisions of the people. it's not today. the position of pressure, threat and blackmail on the united kingdom following the sovereign expression of the popular will to leave the eu is unacceptable and reveals the intentions guiding the eu institutions in this process. among these intentions - which we vehemently denounce and combat - is the attempt to carry out an operation to whitewash the eu's policies and their consequences, and from there to force a breakthrough in the deepening of european capitalist integration. the negotiating process which is now beginning must be fair and balanced, based on the fundamental idea of respect for the sovereign decision of the united kingdom. the defence and protection of the rights of both british citizens residing in eu member states and eu member state citizens residing in the uk should be a priority in these negotiations. rights such as: right of residence, right to equal treatment and non-discrimination, right of access to public services and social security, right to family reunification, international recognition of university diplomas and training. the positive provisions on the free movement of persons must be protected.
in writing. for this parliament there are very clear and necessary conditions for the approval in 2019 of a possible agreement between the eu and the uk, and it is to this excellent resolution that we will return to analyse, point by point, whether they are fulfilled. highlight three: - it cannot be the citizens - european or british - who pay the bill. in particular, both sides need to have the interests and rights of european citizens living in the uk and vice versa at the centre of their concerns. - the eu can never allow the uk to become a tax haven and the fight against tax fraud and evasion will be at the forefront of any trade agreement that is drawn up. - i know . in all forms of cooperation that may exist between the eu and the uk in areas that favour the interests of both parties - cooperation to which this parliament and the europe of 27 remain open - the compass will be, as in any union's external action policy, our values and principles, particularly with regard to respect for international obligations to protect refugees and migrants, and human rights in general.

on behalf of the gue/ngl group, madam president, in october 2015, when we discussed the european single market for electronic communications here, we recalled the warnings made by several national regulators that, in view of the dominant tourist flows in europe, the southern countries would be harmed by the end of roaming charges in the european union. we warned then that there was still no convincing response to these warnings. a year and a half later, we're still doing it. without a convincing answer, without the certainty that consumers in the southern countries will not pay the costs of the end of roaming in the european union in their domestic tariffs. we have intervened in this process with a clear objective: to make it possible to end retail roaming surcharges, i.e. to end roaming charges for consumers in the european union, but at the same time eliminating the risk that costs arising from roaming, in particular costs arising from the increase in seasonal pressure on national networks, are reflected in national domestic charges. in our view there were and are more effective ways of ensuring this than the provisions which have been adopted. but it was the choice of the commission and the rapporteur not to take them into account in this regulation. the logic of liberalization and the miraculous free competition underlying the single market in communications, in this as in other sectors, i conclude, serves the interests of large economic groups and monopolistic concentration on a european scale. it serves neither the interests of consumers nor the national interest.
madam president, as from 15 june, european citizens will be able to access data, voice and text without paying roaming charges, provided that their consumption is in line with normal standards. they will thus be able to access telecommunications services from anywhere in the european union as if they were at home, and this is an excellent measure. but, commissioner, it must be a measure applied fairly and effectively, and for that there are two conditions. first, national operators, on the basis of the rule allowing for the recovery of losses proven as a result of the measure, cannot allow operators to impose on non-traveling citizens costs resulting from the response to the demand of displaced citizens and, second, european incentive schemes for investment in digital networks must give priority to territories that will have to increase their capacity to respond to seasonal demand. there are instruments and procedures in this report, which i myself have endorsed, to ensure that both these conditions are met. it is essential to ensure that this is an effective and fair measure.

member of the commission. – mr president, the belarus debate in the european union continues to be driven by a policy of critical engagement. the most recent events in the lead-up to freedom day in belarus on 25 march confirmed the need to maintain a critical approach in our bilateral relations. in reaction, we issued a statement pointing out that, despite the international community’s calls for restraint, the response by the security services, including preventive arrests beforehand and the detention of a large number of peaceful protesters on 25 march, itself were indiscriminate and inappropriate. we further noted that such limitations to the freedom of expression and assembly are in contradiction with belarus’ stated policy of democratisation, as well as its international commitments. we called for the immediate release of all recently detained peaceful citizens. belarus has taken a number of positive steps in recent years. these include the pro-active participation of belarus in the eastern partnership and in the interim phase on modernisation issues; the resumption of the eu-belarus human rights dialogue; the start of negotiations on visa facilitation, readmission agreements and a mobility partnership; the active pursuit of cooperation in the harmonisation of digital markets; and the signature of a cooperation agreement on an early warning mechanism in the energy sector. belarus has also played an increasingly constructive role in the region, notably with regard to the conflict in eastern ukraine. after careful consideration, and with the support of member states, including the local heads of mission in minsk, and following the discussion in the political and security committee, it was decided to proceed with an eu-belarus coordination group meeting on 3 and 4 april, understanding that this was an opportunity to pass strong and unambiguous messages to belarussian interlocutors at an appropriate level. a specific session of the meeting was dedicated to human rights and political freedoms. this is exactly what we mean by critical engagement. formats, such as the coordination group and the annual human rights dialogue, allow us to reiterate our commitment to a stable, democratic and prosperous future for belarus for the benefit of its people. a range of subjects are of particularly importance to civil society were discussed at the meeting, including mobility, people-to-people contacts, trade, education and social security. the european union also raised issues related to the registration of political parties, as well as the reinstatement of the civil and political rights of former political prisoners. in addition, separate meetings took place with belarussian human rights defenders, civil society representatives and members of political opposition movements. the european union will also raise these issues at the next human rights dialogue, which is scheduled to take place before summer. as stated in the council conclusions of 15 february 2016, the european union is open to the further development of eu-belarus relations and to enhancing political relations and sectoral cooperation in the appropriate context. at the same time, tangible steps taken by belarus to respect universal fundamental freedoms, the rule of law and human rights will remain key for the shaping of the eu’s future policy towards belarus. we take a note of today’s resolution on belarus and share the concerns expressed. we will continue to closely observe belarus’ response, with regard to freedom of speech, assembly and association, as we take our policy forward.
in writing. other on 25 march, in minsk, belarusian riot police violently suppressed the opposition's annual march, beating people, preventing journalists from doing their work and arresting about 700 protesters. the march was aimed at protesting against the law against social parasites which created a special tax of 220 euros for those who work less than six months during the year and contribute less to the financing of the state. the promoters of this peaceful march wanted it to coincide with freedom day, the unofficial date of the celebration of the first belarusian people's republic in 1918. but 'freedom' is not a word for president lukashenko, who has ruled with an iron fist since 1994, with a long record of repression of peaceful demonstrations, arrests for political reasons and the shameful title of being the only country in europe still applying the death penalty! on the same day, we celebrated the 60th anniversary of the treaty of rome and reaffirmed our commitment to the values of our founding fathers. it is in the name of these values that we need to review the framework of our relationship with belarus and make it conditional on respect for human rights, fundamental freedoms and the rule of law.
mr president, the government of bangladesh has committed itself to concrete targets to combat child marriage, a scourge that leaves terrible emotional and physical consequences, destroys normal personality development and brings with it an increased risk of poverty, illiteracy and sexual and physical violence. bangladesh has made some progress in this area, although it remains the country with the highest rate of human rights violations in the world. however, the recently passed law restricting child marriage, instead of being a decisive step to combat this tragedy, is a perverse law that introduces exceptions in special cases or in the interest of the adolescent, without clarifying the criteria that govern these classifications and without establishing a minimum age for the marriages in question. bangladesh is a beneficiary of the un population fund and unicef global programme to accelerate the fight against child marriage, which is co-funded by the european union. the european union must therefore make efforts to put pressure on the bangladeshi authorities to review the law restricting child marriage, otherwise we will see a setback in the fight against this practice, which is the cause of so much suffering.

mr president, the proposals you have brought to us today are a perfect example of how the firmness with which you defend sanctions and political impositions on sovereign countries is always lacking when it comes to proposals which serve the interests of the workers and the people. the unrelenting truth that reality will demonstrate is that the strategies presented, which are supposedly of a social nature, are nothing but instruments for levelling the rights of workers and peoples below the benchmark. in fact, the portfolio proposals in the field of maternity are an example of this. they throw out the proposals of this parliament for extending the periods of leave and paying for leave without loss of pay, they run away like the devil from any reference to public policies or the provision of free services. it is your political choices that fuel inequalities between men and women and social inequalities, as well as ensuring that the mechanisms of exploitation and impoverishment of workers and peoples are perpetuated.
replying in accordance with the 'blue card' procedure. generations of poor people are being created at the expense of the policies that the european commission, the european union and the majority of this house have been adopting, which in recent years have increased by tens of millions the number of people who are now living in poverty and social exclusion. it is the policies of the european union, the macroeconomic policies, which prevent progressive development policies in the member states and social development policy, which attack public services and the network of public services that defends, for example, maternity, which destroy public services and which lead to the privatisation of these services. these are the policies that are behind the impoverishment as well.
the manifestations of social concern on the part of the defenders of european capitalist integration can be poignant. but they never question the policies that have brought us devastation and social backwardness. the eu's social makeup reaches its highest point with the so-called 'social pillar'. the building that was so anti-social and anti-democratic - with pillars such as the stability pact, the european semester, the budget treaty, economic governance, sanctions, etc. - would eventually become 'social' thanks to the miraculous pillar. i'd almost laugh if it wasn't so serious. the pillar is nothing but the siren call of those who, faced with the insurmountable contradictions of an exhausted and unanswerable integration of the problems of the peoples (which, moreover, has caused and aggravated them), are crying out for more of the same. as far as the substance of the matter is concerned, the pillar does not include the raising of wages and salaries, the defence of collective bargaining or universal and free public services. the aim is to harmonize labour and social legislation (i.e. to level it down) in order to facilitate the movement of cheap labour within the single market. it is a question of legitimising precariousness and the 'social' user-payer principle in access to public services.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, today we are again stressing the importance of the blue economy, of maritime affairs, but more specifically the importance of fisheries for the economic and social development of the outermost regions. we clearly need the commitment of all, political actors, regional and local authorities, to define the future common fisheries policy. today we are stressing the need for a european financial instrument for fisheries, just as in agriculture. we are fighting for a more united union, for a more social europe, for a europe which respects and fulfils its treaties. we shall have it if we do not fail to take action against the outermost fishermen, who are also european citizens. support for the renewal of outermost fishing vessels is without prejudice to the common fisheries policy and the emff. these are vessels with specific characteristics and constraints. renewing them is about ensuring the safety of those who work at sea, it is about improving the quality of fish, it is about protecting the marine ecosystem. count on the support of all political parties for this cause, certain that they will not disappoint the citizens who elected us here to defend them.
i would also like to congratulate the rapporteur, ulrike rodust, on her hard work and dedication. the outermost regions are distinct zones and are therefore the subject of a specific article in the treaty on the functioning of the european union. the initiative report we are discussing here, based on this legal background, rightly calls for the creation of a financing instrument which differentiates these regions. in order to allow public aid to be granted for the replacement of vessels, an amendment calling for this possibility will be voted on tomorrow. it is important that public aid can be granted to fishermen in these remote areas and this does not affect the common fisheries policy, it just requires the amendment of a european commission recommendation. in addition, public aid for the replacement of vessels is prohibited by a commission recommendation, not by the basic cfp regulation. a recommendation which has not even been passed by the european parliament or the european council.
the report recognizes that the common fisheries policy does not respond to the specificities and needs of the outermost regions. it is a positive step, but it now requires at least legislative amendments to correct what is wrong. we welcome the proposal to create a new posei-fisheries, which we have been advocating for a long time and which we consider essential to combat the permanent disadvantages and constraints facing these regions. as regards the need to modernise and renew the fishing fleet in these regions, we must go further and therefore support the amendment which goes in this direction, which seems to us to be extremely well thought out and moderate. we shall continue to fight for the extension of the exclusive reserve area for the fleets of each region to the boundary of the exclusive economic zone, that is, from the current 100 to 200 miles. we will also continue to support measures that favour small-scale fisheries in the interests of environmental, social and economic sustainability.

madam president, i voted in favour of this report because i believe that sustainable fishing using traditional fishing gear is the basis for the prosperity of coastal communities and contributes to food security in the outermost regions. the outermost regions are in a unique situation within the european union. in a natural context marked by insularity, a tropical climate and a rugged volcanic terrain, these regions are very far from the european continent, as is the case in my country, the azores and madeira. but they are of historical importance, they play a strategic role in protecting europe's sea lanes and defending its external borders. in recent years, the fishing pressure in the exclusive economic zone of some octs has increased to between 100 and 200 miles, with fishing being carried out predominantly by fleets not belonging to the octs concerned. the european union, geographically spread over four seas and two oceans and surrounded by islands, must protect its marine biological resources and create measures to support its small-scale and traditional fishing vessels, on which the survival of many fishing communities in the ors depends. we must not be fooled, what is at stake here is the survival of the fisheries sector in the ors.

mr president, we have known for some time that the so-called globalisation, made possible by the achievements of science and technology, is not politically neutral. this globalisation corresponded to the expansion and consolidation of capitalist relations of production and the assertion of capitalism as the dominant economic and social formation on a planetary scale. the effects of this globalisation are the effects of the system that produced it. its contradictions are also the contradictions of the system that produced it: the contradiction between the social character of production and the private appropriation of the means of production, the contradiction between a system that either grows and accumulates or dies and the limits of a land that, being generous, is finite. the increasing concentration of wealth in the hands of a tiny minority has, on the other side of the coin, the globalization of the exploitation of man and nature, poverty, exclusion, illiteracy, disease and war. the european union is an agent of this globalisation. socialism is the only way out.
