mr president, commissioner, the european fund for strategic investments has undoubtedly made a positive contribution to overcoming the serious investment deficit in the european economy. the socialist group, which called for this instrument, is naturally pleased with the progress made. but after a year and a half, it must be acknowledged that the juncker plan, despite recent improvements, is still far short of what is needed and, above all, has been very badly distributed. the data available also reveal a very high geographical concentration which benefits the most powerful economies and is further aggravating the divergence within the european union. this is particularly true for infrastructure and innovation investment projects, which concentrate almost two thirds of investment support in three member states. this is a very serious problem which needs to be corrected by reviewing the rules, criteria and procedures. the juncker plan will only be a truly european project if it becomes an instrument for convergence in europe, and for that, much will still have to change.
madam president, the european fund for strategic investments is today the mirror of the contradictions within the european union: a contradiction between the major objectives proclaimed by the european commission and the council and the lack of financial means to promote economic recovery and territorial cohesion. so instead of a comprehensive public investment plan to stimulate private investment, we have moved forward with this juncker plan which fails to respond to the real challenges of the present and the future. nothing moves us against private investment. what we criticize is this promiscuity of these public-private partnerships where the private invests and keeps the profits, while the public takes the risks and the eventual losses. this plan has been in existence for just over a year. the available assessments confirm the criticisms we have made, both in the additional aspect and in the automatic geographical distribution. we are therefore faced with a direct transfer of public funds to the private sector, without any democratic control and whose impact on the investment deficit remains to be proven. in portugal, apart from the eib loan of eur 50 000 000 to the lisbon city council, we have nothing tangible on the ground. to extend this plan and extend its scope to other areas such as defense is to repeat the same mistake. the problem will not be solved by implementing yet another tight central direction in private investment choices. let us instead put our knowledge and experience to work in the service of a public investment plan, in the service of cohesion, development and the quality of life of the people.
the european fund for strategic investments is not, and cannot be, a substitute for cohesion policy and therefore has no geographical quotas. the european fund for strategic investments requires proactivity from its beneficiaries and proactivity from the territories. this does not mean that we should not seek territorial cohesion, geographical cohesion, the distribution of projects. you should look for it. and that is why parliament insists on the advisory hub, the advisory platform to help structure projects and projects of higher quality, to help create platforms, to also contribute to the complementarity that we want to see and that is being strengthened between this fund and the cohesion policy funds. we insist on the advisory hub, this advisory platform to achieve this goal. it is clear that this european fund for strategic investments has contributed to employment; it has also contributed to enterprises, especially small and medium-sized enterprises, and also to entrepreneurship, innovation and the strengthening of the european union's competitiveness. it is also clear that the additional aspect needs to be strengthened and also the selection of projects must take into account the place where the project will be realised. the same project does not have the same economic impact in terms of job creation in a less developed region or in a more developed region. and that is why special attention must be paid to the less developed regions, although i would stress that this fund can never be confused with cohesion policy.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a good example of the need to ensure that the directive is applied in a uniform manner. in 2015, in the eu28, 27% of children and young people, 25.3 million, live in poverty. the eu's austerity policies have forced member states to cut social benefits and earnings-replacement benefits when they were needed most. right-wing and austerity policies that have imposed cuts in health, education and other public services, while imposing wage cuts, unemployment, degradation of labour relations, increasing inequalities and poverty. it is your policies of exploitation and misery that are fuelling the reality that cannot be hidden today in countless countries: thousands of children marked in schools as hungry, basic needs such as clothing, housing and health care not being met, children who are victims of abandonment and school failure, child labour, begging, youth prostitution, abandonment. child poverty requires multidimensional responses, which are only possible in the context of a break with the policies of the european union: among others, increasing employment, combating precariousness, increasing wages and redistributing wealth, protecting family incomes, strengthening the social functions of the state and social benefits, guaranteeing free and quality public services such as health, education, but also public networks of day-care centres and pre-schools.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the recent economic crisis and the resulting austerity have hit families with children particularly hard, particularly the larger ones, single parents and immigrants, and children have been hit hard. between 2008 and 2014, child poverty increased in two-thirds of european countries, reaching the highest poverty rate among all age groups. in the european union, one in four children is at risk of poverty or social exclusion. in my country, portugal, in 2015, a year after the end of the economic and financial assistance program, 536,000 children, almost 30 percent, were at risk. these children who grow up in poverty and in conditions of social exclusion are children whose rights have been stripped away. they are less likely to succeed in school, to enjoy good health or to get a job as adults, thus perpetuating a cycle of social exclusion across generations and making poverty a structural problem. if the europe 2020 strategy aims to lift at least 20 million people out of at-risk-of-poverty, tackling child poverty must be the number one priority of all european social policies. yesterday we celebrated in this house the 30th anniversary of the success of the generation... (the president cut the speaker off)
member of the commission. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, as i said in my opening remarks, child poverty is a phenomenon with multiple causes which the commission and the member states are trying to address. i believe we have the right financial instruments and tools at european level and it is our responsibility to act together at local, regional, national and, of course, european level. i have just mentioned the european pillar of social rights. there is an ambition that proves that this commission puts the future of children at the heart of its priorities. i heard your concerns. i have also heard a shared feeling from all the ranks of this house, and i can assure you that we take everything that has been said here seriously. i said that we want to be concerned about the future of children, and that for a simple reason: because children are, by definition, the future of europe and we cannot, today less than ever, discriminate or leave it alone. we have no right to be indifferent. we all have the duties of the mobilisation.
president-in-office of the council. – madam president, let me just recall the conclusions adopted by the council in march: reducing child poverty and breaking the cycle of poverty and social exclusion across generations require integrated strategies that combine prevention and support. these strategies should aim at supporting parents’ access to the labour market and enhancing preventive approaches through early intervention and increased support to families. thank you once more for this debate; it has shown again the multifaceted and persistent nature of this problem. further efforts are evidently needed from all sides involved.
in writing. at a time when scientific and technological development is reaching unprecedented levels, child poverty is one of the most revolting expressions of the inability of the dominant economic and social formation on a global scale capitalism to meet the most basic human needs, despite the means at its disposal. a recent unicef study stated that social inequalities have deepened with the crisis and have particularly affected children, concluding that there is an absolute need to protect family incomes through social policies. in 2015, 25.3 million children and young people lived in poverty in the eu28. eu policies have forced member states to cut social benefits and supports for families and children and earnings-replacement benefits when they were needed most. cuts in health care, education, and other public services. wage cuts, unemployment, degradation of labour relations, increasing inequalities and poverty. the result: hunger in schools, basic needs such as clothing, housing and health care not met, children victims of abandonment and school failure, child labor, begging, youth prostitution. we need answers that imply a break with eu policies. the fight against precariousness, wage increases, redistribution of wealth, strengthening of the social functions of the state, free and quality public services.
member of the commission. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission agrees with parliament that investment in ports is crucial for our economy. state aid rules facilitate this process more than they hinder it. first of all, i would like to clarify an important point, namely that the state aid rules apply to aid which distorts competition. the commission's control is therefore limited to the economic activity of ports. the commission has neither the power nor the intention to prevent member states from compensating ports for the cost of tasks falling within the prerogatives of public authority. this applies for example to security or - in my portfolio sense - to activities related to customs controls or the police. such compensation is simply not state aid. however, even where the aid concerns economic activities, state aid rules can facilitate investment. the commission has recently defined clear criteria in the general block exemption regulation which allow member states to support certain investments in ports without seeking prior authorisation from the commission. the new regulation applies equally to all ports, whether they are under the control of private or public shareholders. it is based on the commission's well-established decision-making practice in the area of investment aid for ports. in all the cases it has dealt with to date, the commission has approved the state aid after verifying that it was limited to what was strictly necessary to generate the investment. the new regulation now provides member states with more clarity and also more flexibility when granting aid to ports. member states can make public investments of up to €150 million in maritime ports and up to €50 million in inland ports, with full legal certainty now. the public authorities may also finance the costs of dredging, as well as the access waterways. the port services regulation, which entered into force on 24 march, also introduced a requirement for financial transparency, which facilitated the inclusion of port investments in the general block exemption regulation i mentioned. you see, the commission has recently made it much easier to invest in ports under the state aid rules, but, as you know, it is in its role to ensure that there are fair conditions of competition, fair competition, in this economic sector which is very significant. the commission is therefore taking a closer look at corporate tax exemptions for public and private ports. these exemptions are very different from the investment aid which the commission facilitates. they are not targeted at financing certain investments or costs. they are not transparent, nor do they pursue a general interest objective, such as promoting mobility or multimodal transport. in fact, tax saving - and this is the problem - can be used for any type of activity. the commission considers that the exemptions are not appropriate either, since they are not limited to what is necessary to achieve the investment which is, again, the virtuous aspect of the port economy. on the contrary, corporation tax exemptions are proportional to profits. this has the paradoxical effect that small loss-making ports, those which are likely to need the most support, are deprived of any advantage, while large ports, which are the most profitable, benefit the most. it's a bit of a upside-down world, and it doesn't seem justified or fair. as guardian of the treaties, the commission must ensure that fair conditions of competition exist in the internal market. this is why we are looking at corporate tax exemptions for ports in more detail. of course, the commission treats all member states equally when applying state aid rules. the surveys, which started in 2013, showed that most member states subject the economic activities of their ports to the normal corporate tax regime. the commission is currently examining the tax system in several member states where this is not the case. these investigations are not all at the same stage. the reason for this is that the commission was not informed at the same time of the tax measures in the different member states. for example, it became aware of the tax provisions in the netherlands in a wider investigation into the tax exemption for public undertakings, some years before it became aware of similar measures applying to ports in other member states. the commission also concentrated its action on member states where competition problems warranted rapid action. she's made good progress in her investigations of the french and belgian ports. it also made progress in examining possible tax advantages for ports in other member states. no final decision has yet been taken in these cases. i am fully aware, as is my colleague margrethe vestager, whom i represent here, of the demands from this parliament that the commission disclose the member states' answers to our questions on ports and taxation. i would like to emphasise that the information provided by member states to the commission in the course of state aid investigations is protected and confidential, in particular until the commission has brought those investigations to a close. of course, the commission remains committed to explaining its approach to ports to this parliament, as i am doing today. it will inform the european parliament as soon as it has completed the further investigations into tax exemptions for ports. it is also prepared to explain to you how it facilitates investment in ports, as it has done in the general block exemption regulation and in many state aid cases. i therefore thank you for raising this question, which i believe will allow all the necessary clarifications to be made, at least all those that are possible at this stage, and i look forward, of course, with great interest to the debate that will follow.
the commission's proposal is to be seen as a step in the right direction, and it is in the interests of competition and the liberalisation of the single market that the commission takes a leading role in intervening in decisions which are the responsibility of the member states alone, in defining what their sovereign development policies should be. more than the discussion of those decisions, what should motivate the debate are the consequences and the damage to the states that result from the privatisation of a sector strategic for the economy, port activity, or the harmful consequences of this competition in the national context, or the assault that you promote on the working conditions of port workers, in particular the dock, imposing precariousness, stealing rights and wages, while the companies operating there accumulate profits for the shareholders, which are not reinvested in the development of the country. the commission may well wish to take the role of a champion of justice: we know the responsibilities it has in imposing austerity policies, privatizations, the destruction of the productive machinery of countries and the assault on workers' rights.
madam president, on 29 may last, journalist afgan mukhtarli was abducted by unidentified men who were allegedly wearing georgian judicial police uniforms, and later released to the azerbaijani authorities. afgan mukhtarli was beaten and driven to the azerbaijani border with a large sum of money, with the sole purpose of allowing the azerbaijani authorities to prosecute him for illegal border crossing and smuggling. the evidence was manipulated to allow his arrest and conviction to a prison term of several years. the truth, ladies and gentlemen, is that this practice of intimidation, repression and persecution is applied repeatedly in azerbaijan to the leaders of non-governmental organizations, human rights defenders, members of the opposition and journalists. the european parliament must condemn unequivocally the arbitrary detention of journalist afgan mukhtarli and demand his immediate release at a time when negotiations are under way to implement the new partnership between the european union and azerbaijan. i believe that the european union cannot make any pact with regimes which blatantly and repeatedly violate human rights and fundamental freedoms and must do everything possible to ensure that violations of international law are duly punished.
despite the release of human rights activists in azerbaijan over the past two years, the crackdown on independent media, attacks on freedom of expression have become increasingly brutal, including imprisonment, fabricated criminal charges, intimidating surveillance, torture and even death in state custody, as happened to blogger mehman huseynov last april. the refusal of the azerbaijani government to comply with judgments of the european court of human rights is also intolerable. we strongly condemn the abduction of afgan mukhtarli in georgia and his forcible transfer to a prison in azerbaijan. whoever was involved in this kidnapping must be investigated and brought to justice in georgia, a country that should welcome all asylum seekers fleeing political persecution in neighbouring countries such as azerbaijan, turkey or russia. afghan mukhtarli and all other political prisoners in azerbaijan should be released immediately and pending charges should be dropped. i hope that the requests of commissioner johannes hahn in baku will be met by the azerbaijani government now, not in months. here in this parliament there are many of us who will not trade human rights for oil or for bribes from the kleptocracy that oppresses the people of azerbaijan.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, three days ago the pakistani taimoor raza was sentenced to death for having published several comments on facebook in which he allegedly blasphemed the prophet muhammad and his wives. since the government approved the abolition of the moratorium on the execution of the death penalty three years ago, pakistan has become one of the countries currently applying it the most. but this is the first time she has come across a defendant for committing blasphemy on social media. this condemnation is also another sad example of the total instrumentalisation of the blasphemy law for the repression of religious minorities, for the silencing of intellectuals and human rights defenders and for the encouragement of violent revenge against the accused and against their families and defenders. the lynching of mashal khan two months ago by his university colleagues illustrates this well. the growing wave of repression in the country, including against humanitarian aid groups and non-governmental organisations, cannot be ignored by the european union at the next bilateral summit and should condition progress in the implementation of the strategic cooperation plan.
a muslim-majority democracy in the world's fourth most populous country, a diverse and open society: this is indonesia, which has strong relations with the european union. and so, as friends and partners of indonesia, we are concerned about the arrest of governor ahok for blasphemy and the growing incidents of intolerance against ethnic, religious and sexual minorities and the restriction of the freedoms of the majority of citizens, who are women, fomented from abroad by fundamentalist currents financed by saudi arabia and other gulf countries and from within by agents nostalgic for the militarist and repressive suharto regime. the resolution encourages president widodo and all indonesian authorities to continue and redouble their efforts to combat attempts to restrict democratic freedoms and respect for diversity which are inherent in the national motto 'bhineka tunggal ika' and the pancasila philosophy of religious tolerance. it is unacceptable that sharia law in aceh serves to oppress women and lgbti minorities and violate other basic freedoms hard won with democracy in indonesia. it is intolerable that medieval practices of public flogging are introduced under the pretext of a perverted pseudo-religious version that has nothing to do with the exemplaryly tolerant form of islam as practiced by the majority of muslims in indonesia.

mr president, after the democratic opening which the fall of the authoritarian suharto regime represented in indonesia, we are now witnessing a worrying setback in the guarantee of human rights in what is the largest muslim country in the world. in this country, women are still placed in situations of extreme submission and half of the girls are victims of genital mutilation, homosexuals and lgbti people suffer all kinds of mistreatment, arbitrary arrests and detentions and the blasphemy law has been used as a pretext for violating the constitutional protection enjoyed by the various hindu, buddhist and, above all, christian religious minorities. the european union cannot but condemn these violations and make the negotiations for a free trade agreement with indonesia conditional on the fulfilment of the international human rights obligations binding on that country, including the reinstatement of the moratorium on the death penalty with a view to its abolition and respect for the very motto of the indonesian shield which we europeans also share: 'united in diversity'.
madam president, the worsening epidemics of hiv/aids, tuberculosis and hepatitis c in europe require the implementation of targeted, effective and innovative strategies based on three key words: prevention, diagnosis and treatment, which must be accompanied by consistent health education programmes. in the field of treatment, it is imperative to ensure access to innovative medicines. this requires addressing the interests of multinational pharmaceutical companies and defending the interests of patients and states. in portugal, active and systematic infection screening of injecting drug users and risk reduction education have resulted in a change in drug use patterns. portugal has been a pioneer on several fronts in the fight against drug addiction and associated communicable diseases, with the implementation of innovative legislation, structures and programmes that have produced results. an achievement that the years of the troika and their governments have come to call into question. in relation to new hiv infections, the number of late diagnoses, particularly among heterosexuals, is a cause for concern and there is a need to increase early diagnosis and reduce the undiagnosed fraction of those infected.
madam president, the capitalist mode of production is in constant conflict with the physical limits of a planet which, being generous in resources, is finite. the constant reduction in the life span of products is a manifestation of this confrontation. a system that either grows and accumulates or dies is by definition not sustainable. in the face of this evidence, some now want to give the system a cleansing bath in the cauldron of miraculous circularity. the so-called 'circular economy' is not the panacea some people want to make it out to be. the conversion of waste into resources may be more or less but it is never total. in this context, legislative initiatives to impose greater durability on products, to combat planned obsolescence, to promote the repair market and the existence of extended commercial guarantees, to make the use of materials subject to conditions such as recyclability, among other measures, are welcome and necessary.
mr president, we are strongly committed to denouncing and ending impunity for any and all crimes against humanity, war crimes, crimes of aggression, or any other. we cannot, however, accept the cynicism and hypocrisy of this resolution, which presents human rights in a manipulated and biased way, defining countries as political targets for geostrategic and economic interests. the plan is skewed and the targets the same as always. the european union and several of its member states have long been examples to the world of the worst atrocities against peoples, including on european soil. the rhetoric around the international criminal court is directed against what should be sovereign decisions of the people and grossly omits its role in whitewashing history such as the nato bombings in yugoslavia, where milosevic's innocence was recognized ten years after his death.
in writing. we welcome the many positive points that the report makes, such as the defence of recruitment and collective bargaining, the need to strengthen representative structures and trade unions and their active involvement in the definition of labour policies and legislation, or the increase in supervision and the fight against precariousness, or the defence of free and quality public services, particularly in health and education. we reject, however, in the strongest possible terms the intolerable notions that the right wing has tried to introduce, from labour flexibility, the individualisation of rights, to the validation of precariousness as a necessity in the interests of employers. precariousness exists, it is regulated by your labour policies and it is an instrument of exploitation of the workers! we also denounce the alignment of the right and social democracy to dilute or delete from the document important contributions to equality between men and women, which resulted from the opinion adopted by the committee on women, in particular the reasons and factors of discrimination against women, such as exploitation, precariousness and poverty affecting women in particular, or the need to increase the rights and duration of maternity protection, with 100% paid leave and free public facilities such as day care and pre-schools, or a firm fight against discrimination on the grounds of sex.
mr president, i should like to congratulate the rapporteur, renata briano, on the initiative she has taken in this report and on its final content. diversification of yield is essential for fisheries in europe. on the one hand, many springs are depleted, and on the other, fishermen are struggling to recover their income. we need to find sustainable solutions, and this could be one of them. under strict safety conditions, the commercial artisanal fishing vessels of the azores - the region from which i come - can operate with tourists on board. the fisheries industry and the catering industry are now working together, allowing visitors to watch and assist in the catching and then learn how to prepare the fish, in keeping with the tradition of this outermost region. this is a process coordinated by the association of women in fisheries of the azores. i think that the azores' example can and should serve as a model for other parts of continental and island europe.
mr president, the deep crisis in which the fisheries sector is found in various countries and regions, and in particular the small-scale fishing sector, has concrete causes that can be clearly identified. the failure of the eu's common fisheries policy in its various dimensions is due to: the commission has not been able to guarantee a decent income to fishermen, it has not contributed to the socioeconomic development of coastal regions, it has not ensured the supply of fish to the population in the context of good conservation of resources. it is in the context of this outright failure that many of the theories of diversification emerge, which, moreover, have parallels in other sectors such as agriculture. without calling into question the positive aspects that may result from activities that, alongside fishing, are complementary (and not alternative) to those carried out by fishermen, as is the case with tourism, it should be stressed that the primary objective and function of the fisherman is to fish. in order to be able to continue to do so for many years to come, what we need first and foremost is a change in policies.
mr president, at the end of april a proposal for an interinstitutional proclamation on the european pillar of social rights was presented. the 20 proposals are concrete and can be seen as a real manual for exploiting and levelling down labour and social rights. they promote the deepening of exploitation and precariousness by means of flexibility, attack the right to social protection and to public and universal social security, align the retirement age with life expectancy, defend precariousness and the submission of social and labour rights to the wishes of employers, big capital and the interests of the executive and the powers of the european union. they eliminate any reference to public policies of services such as education or health, they take a mercantilist view of rights whether in social protection, health, education, water, sanitation, energy, transport, among others. whoever wants them, pay them. the exploitation manual which urges the rejection, and with it the policies and constraints of the european union which promote poverty, inequalities and the suffocation of sovereign peoples and countries.

blue-card question. – mr rosati, do you not feel ashamed to invoke competitive disadvantage for multinationals when our citizens are demanding fairness in taxation, and when we see that there has not been any fairness and, indeed, that multinationals have been abusing our inability to deliver that fairness?
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, madam president, transparency is the best weapon against tax fraud and tax evasion today. in this respect, this country-by-country report should be a fundamental step towards allowing public scrutiny of the behaviour of multinational companies in this area. it so happens that once again, the mountain has given birth to a mouse. the absurd limit of 750 million euros in turnover has been maintained, which leaves the vast majority of multinational companies out of the picture. we could have gone further in the information to be included in the report, but more serious than that is the inclusion at the last minute of an exception clause which will in practice completely neutralise the directive. under the false pretext of protecting commercially sensitive information, a derogation is introduced which allows multinational companies to conceal relevant information, and this exemption can be renewed ad eternum. we cannot take part in this farce. consensus cannot be achieved at the expense of abandoning the fundamental principles for which we were elected. the people, the workers, the eternals sacrificed by austerity policies deserve more and better.
madam president, just like natural persons, companies as legal persons have aspects of their lives, of their activities, which must be protected from public scrutiny. in a world where competition is increasingly intense, no one will dare to question this right to secrecy or confidentiality. however, this cannot be used to conceal the breach of obligations towards society and the state, in particular those of a fiscal nature. the right to reserve should only be guaranteed when it is properly linked to the duty of transparency, i.e. the obligation to publicly demonstrate that tax and social obligations are actually being met. a crime is always a matter of public record and as such cannot be hidden from the scrutiny of the state and society. this balance between the right to reserve and the duty of transparency is one of the prerequisites for healthy economic and social development.
the european parliament has long called for legislation to make multinational groups disclose information on profits, turnover, taxes, employees and other relevant information, broken down by country. the aim is to allow public scrutiny of tax avoidance strategies, to shed light on profit shifting techniques and to dissuade multinationals from aggressive tax planning schemes. in addition to the astronomical losses in tax evasion and fraud for national budgets and the european budget, i would remind you that the commission has found that smes pay 30% more in taxes on average than large multinational companies that use aggressive tax planning. it is therefore absolutely regrettable that the epp, the ecr and the liberals have surrendered to the unspeakable interests of the multinationals. they come here to invoke the oecd and beps when they know perfectly well that nothing in this area is binding. they have the opportunity to apply and pass binding legislation here and they betray the interests of transparency, they betray the interests of the citizens, because the truth is that by allowing a safeguard clause they are serving the interests of the multinationals against the transparency, against the fairness, as regards the tax justice that the citizens demand.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, we know that now is a very important time to prepare the commission work programme and set the right course for the european union. this year is particularly important because we are also discussing the future of europe, and parliament should be involved in these discussions taking place everywhere. we are in a new situation. this is a kind of moment of truth, of clarification, because the right-wing political parties have decided to table their own resolution – a very conservative one, as we see it. let me be precise. what is really needed in the european union today? progressive forces, and i believe the majority of citizens, are sure that we need to have a new growth and jobs strategy driven by sustainable development goals. this is very clear. progressive forces and the majority of citizens know that we need a stronger social pillar, making sure that everybody – in whatever kind of job, including in the digital-economy transformation – has a decent labour contract and full access to social protection. we also need to make sure that we have the means to invest in people, particularly in children with the ‘child guarantee’. well, i must say i find it regrettable that the motion tabled by the conservative forces in this parliament does not agree with any of this. they do not include these requirements – which are so clear – in their motion. we also know that in order to really respect the paris climate deal we need to translate our commitments into a real investment policy supported by the union budget, and we know that in order to cope with the current refugee crisis the real solution is a european asylum system, which requires full reform of the dublin regime. this is so clear. nevertheless, it is not recognised by the right-wing coalition which is now organising the european parliament. finally, we know that, in order to cope with these external challenges, we need to strengthen internal cohesion among europeans, and this means tackling the divergence in the eurozone, with the banking union and fiscal capacity being introduced. it also means having a forward-looking community budget, counting on our own resources, based on corporate taxation, and based on energy emissions. we need to turn europe to the future and not to the past. i believe that parliament must be involved in the discussion which is coming in the european council and everywhere. this is the purpose of our motion and we believe we can get substantial support.
mr president, debt, the euro, banking, three key areas of action and the necessary break with the constraints of the european union. the debt response requires the initiation and support of processes for the renegotiation of public debt, in terms of its maturities, amounts and interest rates, along with the cancellation of its illegitimate component, ensuring that debt service is compatible with the social and economic development of each country. breaking the yoke of the euro, a political instrument of economic concentration and control, which has imposed a brutal dynamic of divergence in the euro zone, requires the creation of support programmes for those countries which consider that their stay in the euro has become unsustainable and unbearable, providing for adequate compensation for the losses caused in the context of a negotiated exit from the single currency. rejecting the banking union means freeing countries from the strategy of concentration, centralisation and the alienation of national banking abroad as a necessary way of regaining public and democratic control of the banking system. no people is condemned to be tied to these or other constraints of the european union which are contrary to its interests and sovereignty, and it will be the will of the people and their struggle that will build the alternative to the future of impoverishment and exploitation that is predicted in any of your scenarios. it will be this struggle that will pave the way to another europe of peace and cooperation, of progress and social justice, of free and sovereign states with equal rights.
madam president, i pay tribute to portuguese sergeant paiva benido, who lost his life in a terrorist attack on a european union mission in bamako with vice-president mogherini. in portugal last week there was a serious theft of military equipment that could be used for terrorist purposes not only because of the lack of investment in security and defence in a country where the state has been disarmed in all its dimensions by neoliberal austerity policies. this is one of the reasons why we absolutely need a european defence plan and to invest in a europe of security and defence, with strategic autonomy to act for our citizens, for international law, for peace, for the responsibility to protect and for conflict resolution globally, in the european framework, nato, the un and other partnerships. to do this, we need a strong defence industrial base, with cooperation and pulling and sharing of resources between member states and european investment in research and technology. for this, the european defence fund must be injected with contributions without further burdening the european budget. we also need to strengthen democratic control, including so that our member states do not continue to foolishly spend taxpayers' money on duplications, overlaps and waste. above all, we need more than president trump to bring us together; we need a european vision and strategic direction, which articulates foreign policy, security and defence, internal security. syria and libya are there to demonstrate the europe of defence that we need and that we have not had so far.
in writing. the european monopolies and the powers that defend their interests, which have colonized markets from the centre to the peripheries of the eu in successive enlargements, and which are throwing themselves out into the world with their known and natural greed, need a state ready to defend their interests, their imperialist drive, their rate of profit. they rely on the great national powers (their home states) but, more than that, on the european superstate (the eu). in the global confrontation over markets, raw materials and spheres of influence, the eu powers need an interventionist and militarized eu at their service. the european commission's 'reflection paper on the future of european defence' sets out the way forward: boosting the military, arms and defence industry and its integration at eu level; more cooperation in defence research and development, new weapon development and external civil or military operations; and closer coordination with nato. the course which the eu symbolises and defends for europe is a danger, a huge threat to the future of the continent and its peoples.

mr president, the budget is a real test of the political honesty of the european union. there is no rhetoric that can cover up the evidence of a declining budget, which is less and less geared to cohesion and which is increasingly fuelling security and defence policies. the text is absolutely clear. expressions such as 'efficiency', 'added value' and 'reforms' are fuelling this idea of doing more with less. we do not believe in the miracle of the multiplication of loaves of bread, and therefore the priorities set out in internal and external security, border control, the fight against terrorism or the european army will be financed by further cuts in cohesion and the common agricultural policy, as happened, moreover, with the present multiannual framework. this debate has the merit of exposing the true nature of this european union and revealing all its political hypocrisy. social and economic cohesion is not achieved by neoliberal reforms, much less by rhetoric. the commission's proposal is to be adopted by the end of the year.

madam president, i voted in favour of this resolution in recognition of the need for an integrated european union strategic framework on hiv, tuberculosis and viral hepatitis. i was the shadow rapporteur on the report adopted in this house on 2 march on the european union's options for improving access to medicines, which calls on the commission and the member states to adopt strategic plans to ensure access to life-saving medicines and to coordinate a plan to eradicate hepatitis c in the european union through the use of instruments such as european public procurement. europe is a space of solidarity and therefore the european union has a responsibility to deliver on the united nations sustainable development goals, in particular goal 3, which includes the goal of eradicating the hiv and tuberculosis epidemics by 2030 and combating hepatitis. parliament has systematically asked the commission about the union's responses to hiv-aids, tuberculosis and hepatitis c. i believe that the commission must take all measures to promote effective public solutions in the prevention of these diseases and i will continue to follow this matter closely so that european citizens have a healthier future and better living conditions.
the repression by the erdogan regime, which has worsened since the failed coup in 2016, is in full violation of the copenhagen criteria. the european union must take action and cannot continue illegal and immoral business which uses refugees as a bargaining chip. the amendments to the turkish constitution, voted in a rigged referendum, violate the principle of separation of powers. we receive countless reports of human rights violations, not only from recognized organizations, but from desperate citizens who write to us directly, disappearances, forced resignations, confiscation of property without judicial basis, media censorship, political prisoners, torture, threats to re-introduce the death penalty. the european union must of course continue to engage in dialogue and relations with its neighbour turkey, including in order to bring an end to the division of cyprus, but it must above all direct support to turkish civil society fighting against repression. there are no conditions for continuing the process of turkey's accession to the european union at this stage. i therefore support the proposal to formally suspend the negotiations, with the hope and desire that turkey will reverse the disastrous trajectory on which it has been embarked by erdogan's greed for power.
mr president, during the arab spring, turkey was held up as an example of the compatibility of islam and democracy. but the failed coup of 2016 gave president erdogan the pretext to destroy the bastions of secularism and democratic institutions and, under a state of emergency that still exists today, to fire more than 130,000 people and jail public officials, journalists, judges, military and opposition politicians. the tangential victory in the referendum in april this year, the only thing it did was institutionalize the transformation of the turkish parliamentary system into an authoritarian presidential regime, where the head of state accumulates executive functions, and can govern by decree, dissolve the parliament and appoint most of the members of the high council of judges and prosecutors. ladies and gentlemen, it has been many decades since we have seen such a descent into authoritarianism and such a disregard for human rights and the values of freedom and the rule of law in europe. this drift and the new constitution run counter to the copenhagen criteria and therefore, without prejudice to the need for constructive dialogue, the negotiations for turkey's accession to the european union should be suspended and pre-accession funds channelled towards supporting civil society and improving the situation of the three million refugees in this country.
mr president, the innovative and open european fund for sustainable development, by mobilising eur 44 billion for africa and the eastern neighbourhood, will enable the beneficiary territories to achieve more economic growth, more employment and stability. it is also essential to ensure geographical and thematic diversification in the approval of projects and platforms. this geographical diversification should also allow, at the same time, to ensure the reduction of poverty and the promotion of human rights. small projects and small and mediumsized enterprises must be cherished. we are not always aware that the european union is the world's largest donor. but we also have to simultaneously promote entrepreneurship, well-being and financial independence of the people. sustainable projects should create jobs, especially for young people. this fund, while respecting additionality, should be used in complementarity with other financial instruments and also with the european development fund. the european commission should support the designated second pillar and should support the development of investment platforms. it is also essential to have transparency in management, transparency in the approval and then also in the monitoring and implementation of projects. this is an imperative that cannot be forgotten. parliament should also continue to be associated with this important fund and be informed of the adoption of the platforms and projects.

mr president, the sustainable development goals, 17 goals comprising 169 targets and more than 300 indicators set by the united nations in rio in 2012, justify this debate. it has two dimensions: the objectives themselves and the european union's attitude towards these objectives. the objectives range from poverty eradication to the protection of ecosystems, covering a multiplicity of areas. these are generally relevant and fair objectives, although a certain bias is evident, for example in over-valuing the role of economic growth, since, as regards the role of the european union and advertising aside, consideration of its general guidelines and sectoral policies leads to the conclusion that they will not result in the desired sustainability. sustainability requires breaking with free and unregulated trade, with the generalization of intensive production models, with the deregulation of the financial sector and the free movement of capital, with the erosion of social and labour rights, with the attack on public services, just to give a few examples.
mr president, it is obviously not for the council or the commission, or even for this parliament, to decide on this matter. the decision is taken by unanimity of the member states to amend the treaties and to allow the determination of a single seat for the european parliament. however, i believe that we should take advantage of the election of the french president - the most pro-european of the candidates in the french elections - and the brexit negotiations to relaunch the debate on the revision of the treaties so that this house can meet in one place. it is not a question of launching any campaign against strasbourg, the symbolic site of franco-german reconciliation and the starting point of european integration. it is a question of putting aside nationalist selfishness and finding a solution which is more efficient from the economic, environmental and parliamentary point of view. the monthly travel to strasbourg of meps, assistants, interpreters and journalists costs around €114 million and represents additional emissions of 19 000 tonnes of co2 per year. these values are not justifiable in the eyes of those we all represent here - the citizens of europe - and who were required to make so many sacrifices in the wake of the 2008 crisis. parliament may not have the power to decide, but certainly, on behalf of those it represents - the people of europe - it can and must discuss this matter. and furthermore, with regard to the european medicines agency, it is proven that portugal is the ideal place to have the agency.
the committee on agriculture and rural development has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. mr president, commissioner, i congratulate the rapporteur, mr younous omarjee, on the initiative he took in this report and on its final content. the development of a socially and economically cohesive and supportive europe is a daily task. the differentiated treatment of the outermost regions by all the union institutions and services is legally established, but requires continuous improvement. as the person responsible for the opinion of the committee on agriculture, i stress the need to ensure that the appropriations for posei are consistent in the next multiannual financial framework. posei has been a valuable aid, recognised by the independent analyses promoted by the european commission. i also stress the need for the eu milk market observatory to include the outermost regions, in an autonomous and independent way, in the assessment of prices and in the establishment of the criteria for defining the crisis.
mr president, commissioner, i would like to congratulate mr omarjee on the final product of this report and say that the european union must take account of the social, economic and structural situation of these regions, which are remote regions because of their insularity, small size, terrain, difficult climates and their economic dependence on a small number of products. we must therefore all work to safeguard the competitiveness and specificity of their traditional industries when concluding free trade agreements between the union and third countries, in an increase in investment achieved through the european fund for strategic investments, in order to respond to the market failures in these regions, but also to the very high rates of unemployment, particularly youth unemployment, in the creation of a specific research programme that will allow the networking of their universities, research centres and innovative companies. the rups have all the characteristics of experimental laboratories. i also believe that the internationalisation of the accessibility of the outermost regions through transport infrastructure and routes is essential. i would also like to say that i am particularly pleased that we are speaking here with one voice on what we want for the ors and we are indeed looking forward to the strategy for the ors as early as next autumn.
mr president, i would like to stress five points in this report which we support. firstly, the creation of a posei transport because of the constraints on the mobility of people and goods in these regions in view of their remoteness and the associated increased costs. secondly, no reduction in the posei agriculture, which is a key factor for the economic and social development and territorial cohesion of these regions. thirdly, protection of sensitive products for the outermost regions in the framework of european trade agreements, the impact of which must be assessed in advance. it is necessary to define clearly what a market crisis is in these regions. the report also argues for the need for the milk observatory to have data on this sector of the ors in an autonomous way. fourthly, the recovery of the posei fisheries fund, whose integration into the emff was a huge mistake, which must be corrected, causing damage to fishermen and leading to the loss of autonomy in the management of this important fund. fifthly, the increase in the application of article 349. there is an increased responsibility on the part of the member states to ensure that the scope of this article is not limited to funding, by acting and influencing so that the commission documents already demonstrate respect for the treaty and not just act to remedy this situation. finally, i would like to commend the excellent work of mr omarjee.
mr president, commissioner, the outermost regions are in a unique situation within the european union, in a natural context marked by insularity, tropical climate and rugged volcanic terrain. these regions are far from the european continent, but have a relevant history: they play a strategic role in protecting europe's sea lanes and defending its external borders. the octs are privileged areas with a potential for activities related to the growth of tourism, blue growth, the exploitation of renewable energies, the development of the circular economy and the valorisation of their rich natural heritage and vast biodiversity. my country, portugal, has a history intrinsically linked to the sea and to maritime expansion. the sea has been the identity matrix of the portuguese people for centuries, and our relationship with the sea throughout our collective history has made it possible to redeem a unique space of knowledge and opportunities for all of europe. i therefore believe that the portuguese, azores and madeira ors represent a unique opportunity for a more sustainable europe by strengthening its maritime vision and dimension. i therefore believe that the union must allow the octs to overcome their difficulties and to take advantage of their unique assets and assets, and at the same time that the member states must take greater responsibility for the use of the union's available instruments to enable them to ensure the sustainable development of the octs. this is the design we want for all these regions and i would like to congratulate mr omarjee on the excellent work he has done on this report.
mr president, the outermost regions face structural factors such as remoteness, insularity and so on which are a permanent constraint on their development. this is the basis for positive discrimination which ensures that these regions have specific measures and provisions to help them cope with these constraints. this positive discrimination has been applied in a very insufficient and limited way by the european union. we therefore agree with the rapporteur's proposal - which i welcome - for the creation of an action plan for the outermost regions to strengthen and extend the effects of this positive discrimination. it is necessary to put an end to the devastating deregulation of markets, to give greater support to regional production, to boost local markets, to support the creation of inter-island markets, for example in the macaronesian region, transport and the mobility of people and goods by creating a posei transportes. it is necessary to ensure that the ors have control over the entirety of their exclusive economic zone, to support research and development in areas such as renewable energy, biodiversity, among others, and among other measures that can and should be integrated into this action plan.
member of the commission. – madam president, as you know, on 17 june, several regions in portugal started to face massive forest fires that tragically resulted in the loss of the lives of 64 people and injured another 210. on behalf of the commission, i would like to express my renewed condolences to the families and the loved ones of the victims and i would also like – this cannot be repeated too often – to pay tribute to the courage and the outstanding work of the brave firefighters who fought the blaze. i know about the firefighters personally. i saw them in many areas on the ground. europe is about solidarity and i am proud that the eu could help portugal at a time of great need. on 18 june, portugal requested assistance through the eu civil protection mechanism. the european union responded swiftly and substantially. within hours, france, spain and italy sent firefighting aircraft and firefighters. greece and cyprus also offered to send firefighters through our mechanism. i would like to warmly thank those countries. i would also like to thank the portuguese authorities for publicly recognising and praising this eu assistance because in that way they communicated the eu solidarity to their citizens. in these critical times, it is our duty to make each citizen aware of what the european union truly stands for. let me mention the specific assistance provided to portugal via the eu civil protection mechanism. firstly, seven aircraft and 135 firefighters with 29 vehicles were sent to portugal. over a week, a total of 747 water drops were carried out. in addition, 61 eu copernicus satellite maps were produced to help assess the damage. through the emergency response coordination centre (ercc), the commission monitored the situation around the clock and was in constant contact with the portuguese and other member states’ authorities. the european union stands ready to support the response to any new forest fire emergency in the eu in the coming months. during the recent forest fire emergency spain too asked our ercc for copernicus satellite maps. emergency management services were therefore activated. as of today, 12 maps have been produced and 14 are in the making. for the 2017 forest fire season, reinforcement measures have also been taken. our ercc is continuously monitoring the situation across europe and civil protection experts from our member states, including spain, are working on our ercc premises in brussels. however, the eu has no assets of its own and no firefighting planes. we rely on our member states to make resources available. on that point, more could be done. as the commission pointed out in february this year in its report on progress and the remaining gaps, the aerial forest firefighting capability should be further strengthened. this is a very important point. but the eu civil protection legislation goes beyond disaster response. as you well know as parliamentarians, it also includes disaster risk management as an equal priority. the commission has recently produced an overview of disaster risks that the eu may face, based on the work carried out by member states in developing national risk assessments, for it is important to have a better understanding of all disaster risks in europe, including forest fires. as the recent tragic events have sadly demonstrated, prevention is always better than cure. the commission provides funding for prevention and preparedness projects and exercises. apart from these activities, the means to fight forest fires in all parts of our continent are to be improved under the annual work programme of the eu civil protection mechanism. in addition, the regional funds also play an important role in addressing climate change adaptation, disaster risk prevention and management, and environmental protection in all our member states. portugal and spain benefit from these funds, which also cover protecting people from forest fires. added to that, both countries receive further support through the cross-border, transnational and interregional programmes with a specific focus on forest fire protection measures.

madam president, violence against women, like domestic violence, has at its root a complex web of economic, social, political and cultural causes which cannot be ignored, which persist and are worsening in our society. these causes are associated and intertwined with psychological dynamics that lead many women into dead ends as a result of feelings of guilt, fear and shame, of responsibility for maintaining the family unit at all costs, of family and social pressure. the successful prevention, combating and eradication of violence against women in its many forms is inseparable from the realisation of women's rights in law and in life, from ensuring equal participation in the private and family spheres as well as in the labour, social, political and sporting spheres, from changing age-old conceptions based on the subordination of women's role in the family, at work and in society, and from promoting a culture based on the values of freedom, democracy, equality, justice and progress.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. mr president, climate change is increasingly present in our lives. we've had the hottest summers, as has been mentioned, tragic fires, floods, hurricanes, and it's the very center of our lives that's at stake. as rapporteur for the itre committee, i have always sought to ensure that we have adequate, robust, sound and fair monitoring in place to enable us to move forward with the objectives of the paris agreement. we have had enormous difficulties in this respect, especially with regard to the reference period. we maintained a division almost to the end, although with agreement on the general issues. but the fact is that it is unacceptable that the interests of industry in just one northern european country can call into question, or put themselves ahead of, the interests of all the countries of europe and their citizens and the common good. i therefore call for a favourable vote on mr lins' report. it is clearly more favourable than the one adopted at itre, which i myself voted against and i must say, commissioner, that i still preferred the commission's version as regards reference periods, but unfortunately this cannot go forward.
commissioner, this proposal determines how carbon dioxide emissions from the agricultural and forestry sector should be incorporated into european climate policy in order to contribute to the reduction of the european union's emissions and is of great relevance in the context of international climate change negotiations because of the political signals it sends about the ambition and determination of the european union in combating climate change. forests are closely linked to climate change. the climate impact of past changes in soil and soil cover is estimated to have accounted for about 40 percent of the total warming between 1850 and 2010, an astronomical figure. it is therefore essential that forests are managed sustainably, maintaining their biological diversity, their productivity, their regenerative capacity, their capacity to fulfil ecological, economic and social functions at local, national and global levels, now and in the future. after one of the hottest summers in history and the severe fires that have hit europe - and my own country in particular - it is important to stress the importance of forests in the fight against climate change. i believe that this proposal - in line with a climate policy that recognises the role of forests and soil as key actors in the fight against climate change - can be the only way to move safely towards a resilient energy union with a forward-looking climate change policy that supports its decarbonisation dimension.
in writing. the adoption of this report is crucial to ensure a strong mandate for the european parliament for the negotiations with the commission and the member states on the eu transparency register for lobbyists. it is essential that the european commission's ethics committee, which looks into violations of the code of conduct, is chosen and can decide independently, and that the grace period between the end of the mandate and acceptance of jobs in the private sector is extended. we also advocate that all relevant commission staff should only meet with entities registered in the transparency register and that these meetings should be published. i also hope that parliament will approve the amendments proposed this week by the rapporteur, which concern the mandatory publication by rapporteurs and shadow rapporteurs of all meetings with interest representatives and the establishment of a grace period which will continue for as long as the temporary transitional allowance is paid, and that any decision on positions taken in the private sector after the mandate will be assessed by an independent authority. it is the least we can do to meet the demands of ethics and integrity of the citizens.
madam president, digitisation is an unstoppable process in society. this is clearly a crucial challenge for university and vocational training. in this connection, it should be stressed that, despite these advances, a significant part of the population still lacks access to these technologies. it should also be noted that there are still economic barriers which restrict access to technology or promote differentiated access between citizens. but above all, we want to stress here that, although we are in favour of technological progress in general and digital in particular, we must create a model that puts these advances at the service of humanity and not at the service of a few oligopolies. unfortunately, the reality shows that the digitisation of the economy has fuelled precariousness at work. digitalization of education can help, but it cannot replace the presence of the teacher, nor can it fuel mass layoffs and more exploitation, and above all, it cannot replace the role of the state in ensuring equal access to university education for all citizens.
madam president, portugal is facing an unprecedented drought situation which requires urgent support. the latest data available from the portuguese institute of the sea and the atmosphere indicate that by the end of june, 80% of mainland portugal was in severe or extreme drought. in addition, 18 of the continent's 60 dams started a summer with less than half of the water they can store. according to the dry weather index, on 30 july the dry weather situation remained in almost the whole territory of mainland portugal, with an increase in its intensity compared to the previous month. in addition to fires, the damage caused by the drastic decrease in crop productivity and the lack of water and fertilizer for livestock is already starting to be estimated at several hundred million euros in various sectors of agriculture and livestock. in this very serious context, we say that it is not just the advance payments of cap aid to farmers that are coming. we must go further, by mobilising the european union's solidarity fund, adapting its regulation so that it can act now, in order to minimise an impending social, economic and environmental catastrophe.
madam president, on 23 august last the brazilian government published a decree providing for the extinction of the cobre and associates national reserve, a territory in the amazon of some 4 million hectares which includes nine protected areas including state forests, ecological reserves and indigenous lands, which immediately gave rise to the massacre of isolated indians in the amazon due to gold prospecting and the slaughter of birds, as reported by the national indian foundation (funai). the subsequent suspension of that order by a federal judge is merely provisional in nature and the second government order, which repealed the first, only opens more loopholes for exploitation in some of these sites. ladies and gentlemen, at a time when negotiations between the european union and mercosur are gaining momentum - and could lead to a political agreement before the end of this year - the european union cannot but urge the brazilian authorities to preserve the indigenous environmental reserves of one of the largest continuous stretches of tropical forest on the planet. the brazilian indians and the amazon, also known as the 'lungs of the world', cannot continue to be constantly threatened by economic interests.
the rapporteur. madam president, commissioner - welcome to parliament - mr president of the european council, ladies and gentlemen, people, all over the world, are increasingly turning to digital technologies and services. digital transformation is now an absolutely unstoppable process, and history teaches us that letting technology decide the future of societies has never worked out well. we must be the ones to decide the future of technology and, above all, we must be the ones to determine what we can do with this technology to make a society more decent, more just, safer, more sustainable, with more opportunities and a better quality of life for people, and this choice is a challenge in which we can and must involve european citizens. this is an opportunity to motivate these citizens to participate in the rebuilding of european society. it's an opportunity to reconnect them with the european project. the wifi4eu initiative is an important step towards achieving this reconnection goal and towards realising this vision. providing all europeans with free and unrestricted access to high-quality internet in public places is a highly symbolic measure that has great political force and has great transformative potential. it is, in fact, such a powerful measure that the president of the european commission used it last year in his state of the union address as one of the anchors of his speech. the challenge that was then given, the most noble moment of the commission's accountability to the europeans, was followed by a stimulating work of mobilization and negotiation that has brought us to this point. networking with the regions, businesses, civil society, institutions and governments has led to the legislation we are debating today. the proposal under consideration guarantees neutrality in the access to the internet. it also ensures that the best technology is selected for each case. it ensures access, regardless of geographical location and also of the income of users. it does not discriminate against any person or any territory. it has a simple application system and a single validation, and therefore wifi4eu is the embryo of a competitive and inclusive european gigabit society. it is, in fact, the embryo of a european digital identity that will result from the inclusion of our common values in the new digital society. i would like to thank all those who have worked with me on this draft report. i thank the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions, which have worked closely with the european parliament, the commission and the council from the outset. i thank the european commission and the council for their convergence in the continuing search for good political, economic, technical and financial solutions. i would like to thank all my colleagues in the european parliament, and in particular the shadow rapporteurs, for the strength and enthusiasm with which they have contributed actively. i thank the technical and political secretariats, who have always been available for a job that was not easy but which has brought us to the right place, opening up new opportunities for people. i therefore call for the adoption of the legislative act under discussion. it is important that the first applications are made this year and that the roots of wifi4eu begin to spread throughout the union. i'm done, wifi4eu is a small step. it is a small step for us, but it is a big step for the european union to take a leading role in developing an inclusive, open, transparent, creative and people-friendly digital society. let us join in this idea for a better future for the european union and the world.
madam president, welcome, madam commissioner, i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteur for his work. i think this report is important for a number of reasons, but above all because it puts a very strong social dimension on what i believe is a daily need. wireless internet access in public spaces free of charge and free is a project of this house of which i am proud and i say it without any problem. we must, however, ensure that, for this investment to be effective, technical support, information and resources must go above all to the most remote areas, to rural areas, to the people and communities who do not have access to it, and i believe that in this dimension we will have to continue to do work. it is important to ensure that access can be financed for everyone and for that reason i once again welcome the work that has been done. but we have to continue to evaluate so that this money, this resource, this technical support, this information does not reach the same people as before, because digital exclusion is a reality. i believe that this report is an important step towards combating digital exclusion and we must continue to work towards this.
madam president, commissioner, it is a pleasure to see you in your new duties. i would like to leave just two brief notes: firstly, to congratulate myself and the commission on the presentation of this proposal. it has a totally innovative voucher and simplified application system, which is able to ensure the financing of free wi-fi equipment in various public spaces. this measure will cover areas in at least 6,000 municipalities in all member states - a number that could rise to 8,000 municipalities if there are sufficient applications. and a second note to welcome the work of this house, the rapporteur carlos zorrinho, who was able to introduce a lot of improvements to the initial document in a very useful joint work led by the itre committee, but which involved three other committees through opinions. in the regi committee, of which i was shadow rapporteur, i proposed a number of amendments to the commission's original proposal, which i am pleased to see included in this report, and i refer specifically to the importance of eliminating language barriers on the access page and the inclusion of the principles of economic, social and territorial cohesion in the wording of this regulation, in accordance with article 174 of the lisbon treaty. i would like to conclude by asking the commission to implement these ideas, which are therefore part of this regulation, rapidly within the union.

mr president, i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that the european union and the member states should strongly support the maintenance of the global moratorium on commercial whaling and the ban on international trade in whale products. in fact, despite the fact that the international whaling commission has imposed a worldwide moratorium on commercial whaling, the truth is that norway continues to hunt whales. in fact, norway even increased its exports of whale meat sharply in 2017. it is noted that whale products are exported from norway to japan via at least three ports in the european union and the european union, by allowing this transit, is facilitating trade in whale species that are protected by european legislation and whose hunting violates the international prohibition of commercial whaling. the commission should therefore assess all possibilities to ensure that whale meat cannot be shipped illicitly through european union ports and the council and the commission should adopt a common approach at future meetings of the international whaling commission on whaling and should establish contacts with third countries in order to achieve a majority in favour of the establishment of whale sanctuaries. with the adoption of this resolution, the european union is sending a clear signal of its rejection of this barbaric and inhumane practice.
