mr president, commissioner, i should like to begin by congratulating the author of this report. this report is of the utmost importance, both in its timing and in its content, and it is unquestionably a report of high quality. this report has a fundamental political objective: to give a signal from this european parliament as to what relations between the european union and latin america should be. in fact, we have no closer partners in terms of the identity of values, principles, models of political and economic organization than most of the countries that make up latin america. this does not correspond to any neocolonial vision, far from it. i do not see in this report any neocolonialist perspective on relations with latin america. what is important is to highlight common values, values which are the values of europe and of the countries of latin america in general, and above all, one thing is important: it is to highlight how much latin america has evolved in recent decades. several democracies have been consolidated, economic development has been taking place, millions of people have been lifted out of poverty, and progress is being made towards consolidating the rule of law. many of the crises that some countries are facing are crises of growth and maturation of their democratic processes, such as the case i know best, for obvious reasons, which is the case of brazil, which is a country that is going through difficulties but which correspond to the consolidation of a democracy associated with a rule of law. i therefore conclude by stressing once again the importance of a report of this kind.
commissioner, in foreign policy, as in almost everything in life, the most urgent is not always the most important. the report we are discussing today reinforces the conviction that relations between the european union and latin america are important, despite the fact that the world is full of conflicts and disputes that are, or are potentially, more prominent in the media and more politically attractive. latin america and the european union, in addition to a strong shared identity, also share the fact that they are victims of deregulated globalization. if we want, and i know we do, to reclaim our global voice and affirm an agenda of sustainability and humanism, we need to work together and give clear signals of a strategic alliance. and trade agreements are a privileged vehicle for translating intentions into action. we have good experience with latin american countries. the conclusion of a trade agreement with mercosur will therefore be a decisive step towards the re-establishment of the geostrategic balance and the consolidation of relations between our peoples.
the close cultural, linguistic, political and historical ties between the european union and the countries of latin america make it vital to develop a shared agenda and to strengthen cooperation in defining the union's major foreign policy priorities. the growing protectionism in the world calls for the strengthening of the union's mutually beneficial bilateral and multilateral economic partnerships in the region, but, beyond the trade side, it is important, in a political context, to strengthen interparliamentary cooperation and to value the continued promotion of democratic values and human rights, including the defence of the property rights of the indigenous peoples of brazil on the lands they have traditionally occupied, the fight against climate change and cooperation in the field of research and innovation. the strengthening of cooperation between the two regional blocs therefore plays a key role in diversifying the economies of both regions and in promoting a more sustainable, more democratic and more inclusive development. and because it is a question of the european union's political relations with latin america, i conclude by invoking the heroic martyred people of venezuela who are dying at the hands of a bloodthirsty regime and by calling on this parliament to do everything possible to alleviate the suffering of this brotherly people.
mr president, this is the moment for us, as a last shot, to have real vision and ambition. what is at stake for this legislature? it is indeed to develop the european house and to move to the next stage, a new floor. it is to make europe not only a single market or a eurozone, but also a political entity able to respond to the new world in front of us. we need the european union to be a big democratic power, supporting our way of life and making sure that we have the means to deliver our values and our way of life. we need new means for peace and security, to manage migration and to support development. but to have this, we need a brand new community budget for the future, based on new own resources; we need to turn to the future and not the past. this means, when it comes to own resources, that big corporations should pay their taxes because while they should benefit from the single market, they should pay what they owe. if we have in mind a new democratic political entity, this can only be based on stronger european citizenship, above all fully respecting the fundamental political rights of our citizens, but also making sure that we can count on updated social standards with regard to access to social protection and to skills, but also making sure that everybody in employment, whatever kind of job they have, can count on a decent labour contract and access to social protection. this should be the ambition of the upcoming european social pillar, but can we say that this europe, as a land of equal opportunity, of fairness, is being delivered to all european citizens? we do not think so. europe is still working as a machine for divergence and inequality and we need to reverse this very undermining trend. so we call on the commission to come up with an ambitious proposal for the european budget, turning the european project into a project for equality and convergence. this should apply to the european union as a whole and also to the eurozone. this is our request for the upcoming work of the commission.
mr president, mr president juncker, first of all, let me greet you and thank you for your speech today, which gives new impetus and encouragement to all of us who, like president juncker, love europe. i was going to challenge you here, but it's not in the list of your immediate concerns, at least not the ones you've presented here. there has been a great wave of fires in portugal this year, with tragic consequences, there have been floods all over europe, even now in poland with tragic consequences, we saw last year several earthquakes in italy, also this year in greece, we know that with climate change there will be many of these accidents. would it not be time to create a genuine european civil protection force which, on the one hand, is closely linked to internal security - it can also have rescue missions in the mediterranean, etc. - but which could, whenever there is a major disaster of this kind, in a few hours, go to the disaster site and help the national forces to combat these disasters and to mitigate their consequences? i think it is time we had a european civil protection force which would in many respects be an embryo of what could become a genuine european armed force.
the eu's responsibilities in the deep crisis in which europe has sunk, in the intolerable imbalances and inequalities between states, have been deliberately concealed in this speech. as it turns out, the intent is to force a breakout. to insist on the same paths, the same policies and the same orientations that have brought us to economic and social disaster. but now in a higher dose. more federalism, more neoliberalism, more militarism, and so on and so forth. the direction that the president of the european commission has come here to announce, speaking on behalf of the beneficiaries and promoters of european capitalist integration, is not an inevitability, nor something that the workers and peoples of europe have to accept. on the contrary, i did. the real future of europe is not limited to the scenarios that the eu now sets for its own future. it is the struggle of the workers and peoples of europe for the right to their sovereign development and for breaking with the constraints which prevent it that will define the paths along which the future of the continent will be built. it will be this struggle that will pave the way for a project of solidarity cooperation between sovereign and equal states, based on mutual benefit, peace and social progress. a project which, as is becoming increasingly clear, requires the defeat of the eu.
juncker's state of the union address, in line with others he has delivered throughout his term, conveyed a positive stance on the future of the european union (eu). i stress the importance attached to deepening participatory democracy and transparency, to the need to build a social pillar suited to the new challenges of the global economy, to european leadership in sustainability and clean and smart mobility systems, to the realisation of the energy union and the digital union, to the extension of the euro to all the countries of the union, to the completion of the banking union and the economic and financial union, to the development of the security union and the defence union and to the new generation trade policies to strengthen the eu's global assertiveness. to achieve this vision, a european union must be internally strong and equipped with instruments to promote convergence. the nine's proof of the importance of this speech will come from the presentation of the financial perspectives in spring 2018. if the necessary budgetary reinforcement is in them, including an increase in the union's own resources, then this state of the union speech will have been a strong moment of consolidation of the european project.
mr president, i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe it is a key element in the realisation of the 2030 climate and energy framework and a crucial step towards a real resilient energy union with a forward-looking climate change policy. climate change is one of the greatest environmental, social and economic threats facing the planet and humanity. every day we see examples of severe and unprecedented weather events that show everyone this truth and this is the time for action. greenhouse gas emissions from aviation activities are increasing significantly. it is estimated that, in the absence of further measures, carbon dioxide emissions from international aviation will almost quadruple by 2050 compared to 2010 levels. ladies and gentlemen, this resolution contributes to the fulfilment of the paris agreement and the ambitious target set by the international civil aviation organisation of global co2 emissions from international aviation not exceeding 2020 levels. the european union continues to lead the fight against climate change and this resolution is an example of this.
mr president, in the debate we had on this report on monday, i called for a vote in favour. the envi committee's commitment was a balanced commitment and today i was forced to vote against this report because of the amendments which were tabled and voted in favour. these are amendments which, once again, promote the lobby of the timber industry, which do not take into account the diversity of the territories, which allow flexibility instead of a common reference period, that is to say, we have put the interests of one or two countries of the european union here because they make the forest their business above the interests of all the european citizens of all the countries. it's true that fires produce emissions. it is true that we have to promote biodiversity. but it's also true that cutting wood, cutting, cutting and cutting for business also produces emissions. with all that flexibility and no reference period, we're at the mercy of the deal, but we lose everybody. i had to change my vote.
mr president, i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that the fight against corruption should be given priority attention. corruption is a complex global phenomenon affecting both north and south and is caused by the inability of political, economic and judicial systems to ensure robust independent oversight and accountability. corruption costs the european union between 179 and 990 billion euros in terms of gdp per year, and, according to the world bank, approximately one trillion dollars worth of bribes are paid annually worldwide. the european union must show strong and unwavering opposition. in this context, the european union should enhance the transparency and accountability of its official development assistance and develop a robust global risk management system to prevent development aid from contributing to corruption in recipient countries. it is about contributing to the direct and explicit fight against impunity through its external policies. i therefore consider it important that the european union should now include an anti-corruption clause alongside human rights clauses in its agreements with third countries.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is regrettable that it took a tragedy at grenfell tower, which took the lives of dozens of people, for us to have this debate for the first time and for the member states and the european commission to take seriously the problems of the safety of our buildings. i have seen this film with the european emergency number and it does not satisfy me that much. the grenfell tragedy happened in london, but it could have happened in lisbon, paris or tallinn. this is a european problem and it must be tackled at european level. fire safety standards vary between member states. some are better than others, but they're all failing. the principle of subsidiarity cannot and should not be a shield against washing our hands and our consciences. a danish firefighter associated with the fire safe europe movement, who is outside this house, has drawn my attention to the fact that while he and his colleagues are confident in dealing with fires inside a room, when it comes to external facades they cannot control the rapid spread of the flames. this is due to the introduction of highly flammable materials into our buildings and the fact that the member states have not updated the regulations on fires, the new materials coming onto the market and also the toxicity of the fumes from their combustion. the bbc has just revealed the high-risk combustion of the chemicals found in the london tragedy. it is a matter of urgency that the commission should take a strong and decisive decision on this matter, and i urge all members to pledge their support to a single objective: the safety of our citizens. against the fire, there's no time to lose. it's time to act.
mr president, in june we watched in horror as the fire that destroyed grenfell tower claimed 79 lives. we now know that the tower was built in the 1970s and rehabilitated last year. however, the new coating was never tested for fire resistance and residents' complaints were never addressed. we all know that prevention is of the utmost importance in managing fire risk. last week i was with firefighters from all over europe who were in parliament urging the european union institutions to act for the safety of buildings and to prioritise building safety standards which would involve more effective methods of fire testing, on the facades, harmonised labelling and improved consumer information, as well as the development of a european strategy aiming at an integrated and coordinated approach in this area. commissioner, if the responsibility for establishing the rules on safety limits in buildings lies with the member states, the life, health and well-being of european citizens is a collective responsibility of all of us, which no principle or rule of subsidiarity can be invoked to remove.
the commission's proposal for a regulation on the statistical information system is a very good one. better statistics can help to better understand reality and therefore to make possibly more informed and consequential decisions. it is true that we agree with the extension of the programme for the period 2018-2020, with its link to the achievement of the 2030 agenda, with the increase of the budget allocated to it, but also with some improvements made to the european commission's project, namely the introduction of employment indicators, which distinguish employment with fixed-term contracts and with permanent contracts and the request that progress be made towards developing a conceptual framework for the production of statistical data series that measure and comply with tax rules, tax avoidance and evasion. however, it is also true that we cannot entirely agree that the focus of this extension is to support 10 political priorities of the european commission such as the european semester, the europe 2020 strategy, the better regulation initiative and the european energy union, on which we have many reservations.

mr president, 30 years after its creation, the erasmus programme is now a world reference in the field of the internationalisation of education and has become one of the targets of the detractors of the european project because it is one of the most successful integration tools of the union, as we hear from the honourable member. the programme has already benefited 9 million young europeans. however, this figure - although impressive - represents less than 5% of young europeans. many are not covered due to socioeconomic factors, funding constraints, inequalities between and within member states, as well as the complexity of the application processes and the lack of consistency in the recognition of the european system for the transfer of credits acquired during the mobility period. it is therefore important that, in the run-up to the negotiations on the next multiannual financial framework, the new erasmus+ programme, to be introduced after 2020, should be able to have the additional funding needed to become more accessible, comprehensive and inclusive, and to lose its reputation as elitist. ladies and gentlemen, we cannot and must not forget that erasmus+ is the greatest investment europe can make in itself.

mr president, earlier this month cambodian opposition leader kem sokha was arbitrarily detained in clear breach of his parliamentary immunity. two days later, he was formally charged with treason by a phnom penh court and faces a sentence of 15 to 30 years in prison. this arrest is the culmination of weeks of political tension that led to the expulsion from cambodia of the american ngo national democratic institute and the closure of 19 radio stations and one of the few independent voices in the written press, the cambodia daily newspaper. what the conviction of kem sokha represents is yet another unacceptable attempt to neutralize the leadership of the main opposition political force, the national rescue party, whose good electoral results in the municipal elections last june now make the hun sen regime fear that the parliamentary elections next july will represent the end of the power it has held in that south-west asian country since 1985. ladies and gentlemen, this house cannot but condemn the detention of kem sokha and call on the cambodian authorities to release him immediately and to respect fundamental freedoms and the most elementary rules of the democratic game.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. – mr president, last week, indeed, was a very sad week for cambodia’s fragile democracy, a sad week for the freedom of the press – as many of you have said – and a sad week for the people of cambodia. the arrest of the opposition leader, kem sokha, on 3 september and the closure of the cambodia daily the day after add to a long list of very serious developments in the country. these include continued restrictions on the freedom of expression, the closure of radio stations, arrests of members of the main opposition party and civil society actors. it is clear that this is a dangerous political escalation. these events suggest a further effort to restrict the space for democratic debate, independent media and civil society ahead of next year’s general elections. the continued use of judicial processes against opposition politicians and ngo figures is widely seen as politically motivated. on our side, as you have recognised, the european union has called on the authorities to release kem sokha immediately. earlier this year we made our voice heard very clearly when cambodia amended the law on political parties twice to introduce provisions that would potentially allow for arbitrary dissolution of political parties. such actions against opposition parties would cast a shadow on the july 2018 elections and ultimately call into question the very legitimacy of the vote. with regard to the elections, the european union has provided support worth eur 10 million to electoral reform, primarily through an improved voter registration process. we have worked closely with the cambodian national election committee in support of electoral reform, mainly through an improved voter registration process. our support has also included technical support on legal and civic education aspects and support to domestic election observation and monitoring of election-related violence. we will monitor closely the updates of the voters’ list that began on 1 september. we also deployed an eu election expert mission to cambodia to follow the local elections last june. the eu delegation, to which some of you have referred, in close coordination with eu embassies and like-minded members of the international community, sent diplomatic observers to polling stations on election day. we have continuously urged the cambodian authorities to take all necessary steps to ensure the conditions for credible, transparent and inclusive elections whose results will command the confidence of cambodia’s voters. the eu has also expressed deep concern over the adoption by the cambodian parliament of amendments, as i said, to the law on political parties, and i have heard your considerations on the possibility of sending a fully-fledged eu election observation mission to the next political elections. if the political situation is conducive to a credible election process and if invited by the government, the european union would be ready to consider the deployment of an eu election observation mission for the national elections due to be held in july next year. i believe that political developments in the coming weeks and months will be a key factor in assessing our possible deployment. the european union and its member states, on another issue that is also important: i have been proud to support the reconstruction and the development of the country from the very beginning. we have an important envelope of development cooperation with cambodia that is embedded in a multi-annual cooperation programme for the period 2014-2020. all our programmes are implemented subject to close monitoring and on the basis of political and policy dialogue with the government. the respect of human rights, democratic principles and the rule of law are fundamental elements in our development policy and are closely monitored in this context. our development cooperation with cambodia is long term, and i have to tell you very clearly that any consideration regarding the possible suspension of cooperation would have to be carefully evaluated in terms of its impact, especially on the poor and vulnerable population, on people’s livelihoods and on better access for children to schools. these are the first priority in helping the cambodian people. a large part of cambodia’s population is still very vulnerable, and we will continue to closely monitor the situation and to use our development cooperation to improve the situation of the population. cambodia has also been granted access to the european markets under the everything but arms scheme, which has served as an important driver of the country’s economic growth. you might be surprised that, despite our geographical distance, the european union is today cambodia’s biggest export market. but we also believe, as i said, that there can be no sustainable development with no human rights and no democratic guarantees, so we are using, and will continue to use, all the available channels to ask, and work, for a substantial change in the current political trends. the european union is ready to continue to work with cambodia towards a consolidation of its democracy and an equitable economic growth, but we look to the authorities of the country to take all the necessary steps to ensure that all political parties, civil society and the media can carry out their activities without fear, as should be the case in any functioning democracy.

on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, we are, of course, worried about the violent situation and the lack of dialogue in catalonia. nevertheless, the problem is larger than this. that is why the s&d group is proposing the following title: this should be a debate and a commission statement ‘on the constitution, rule of law and fundamental rights in spain in the light of the events in catalonia’. this is the title that my group is proposing and it is also backed by other groups.
on behalf of the s&d group, i would first of all like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr gabriel mato, and a number of shadow rapporteurs, including isabelle thomas and, on behalf of envi, renata briano. the work you have done is important and your speed in this case contrasts with the slowness of the commission. it took almost 10 years for the commission to fulfil this obligation, leaving the european union in international default. this transposition of iccat provisions into european law promotes the use of artisanal fishing gear with a lower environmental impact and local vessels. portugal, and in particular the azores, where i come from, has been advocating this strategy because it helps to build and stimulate a fairer, more equitable and more resilient economy. industrial fishing certainly has its place, but it cannot strangle traditional fishing communities. as the regulation clearly provides, member states are called upon to use transparent and objective criteria, including environmental, social and economic criteria, in allocating opportunities and are required to grant incentives to eu fishing vessels using selective fishing gear or fishing techniques with a reduced impact on the environment. i have argued for this point of view in this parliament and in my own country on several occasions, particularly with regard to the allocation of the quota for the azores. as it is now stated, there must be an obligation to try to distribute national quotas fairly among the different segments of the fleet, taking into account traditional and small-scale fishing. the new regulation calls for the extension of the ban on transhipment at sea in non-eu waters and these positions are in line with the fight against illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing. we must maintain the european union's leading position in defending sustainable development and combating iuu fishing. to ensure this, we must strengthen the observer programmes, using the full potential of the data collection regulation which we have recently adopted here. finally, i would like to mention that tuna fisheries in general are in crisis. every good news is followed by bad news. the great tuna schools that once visited ocean islands, such as the azores, annually have stopped doing so. there may be a number of explanations for this, but i am almost certain that the use of fish aggregating devices in tropical waters must bear some responsibility. the use of these devices was rampant in the past but remains excessive at present and, most importantly, these devices continue to be used without the corresponding environmental impact study. in the azores, we fish with line and hook, we refuse to use industrial nets. each fisherman uses only one hook, and yet our fisheries were profitable. not today! they have to solve the problem upstream, making sure that the springs stay healthy and can cross the oceans again. this is in our hands.
mr president, commissioner vella, ladies and gentlemen, as is well known, tuna and tuna-like species are highly migratory species, exploited for commercial fishing throughout their geographical distribution by fleets of different nationalities, with a very varied fishing effort and using very different fishing gear. the sustainability of marine biological resources should be a major concern for all of us. however, i believe that this sustainability must not be blind, that is to say, it must also take account of long-term economic and social sustainability, and in this connection i cannot fail to mention the situation of fishermen and their families, who are in a situation of crisis, of lack of income, even if they are working harder and risking their lives. i am speaking in particular of fishermen in coastal areas, in the outermost regions such as the azores, who are increasingly suffering from a shortage of marine biological resources, namely fish. that is why this proposal is so important, because it is designed to balance all these aspects, especially as regards the tunisians. here too, year after year, both fishermen and our tuna industry see fewer and fewer resources, less tuna, putting in question the sustainability of this important activity in my region, the azores, thus threatening hundreds of direct and indirect jobs because, at a certain point, either for climatic reasons, which led to the change of the migratory routes of the tuna, or for human reasons, with aggressive fishing techniques by others that greatly reduce the individuals who manage to make this migration. the azores' fisheries are therefore part of the cultural and regional heritage and constitute a highly relevant socioeconomic activity. the azoreans are highly dependent on fishing and marine resources play a key role in the local economy and employment, including in the fishing-related industries, notably the tuna canning industry. commissioner, what we are here to discuss today is to harmonize and extend the scope of the iccat recommendations. i wanted to leave two very clear messages here: the first is equal treatment between european fleets and those of third countries. we cannot continue to impose more restrictive and limiting rules on our fishermen than on other fleets which often fail to respect the most basic principles of european standards. the second message is that we are asking you to commit to taking account of the artisanal fleets, especially those in the outermost regions, such as the azores, because we have been an example at european and world level of the sustainability of tuna fishing. in the azores, tuna are caught by hook and line and, in general, live bait, a highly sustainable fishing technique with little environmental impact and very selective. most of the catches are processed by the locally important tuna canning industry, although fresh tuna is becoming increasingly important because it produces higher prices, especially on the export market. finally, i would also ask you to consider the possibility of increasing the quota for bluefin tuna, whose stock is expanding rapidly and which would in fact be very useful in order to compensate european fishermen for the cut in the quota for swordfish, which, as is generally acknowledged, has suffered a sharp deterioration in its stocks. for all the reasons set out above, the increasing adoption of information collected and managed by iccat is encouraged in order to adopt appropriate management measures that safeguard the interests of operators in the sector who practice highly sustainable fisheries, as is the specific case of the azores.
mr president, the iccat convention, which the european union ratified in 1997, has the technical and scientific responsibility for making specific proposals and recommendations for the conservation and management of tuna fisheries. these are acts which are primarily addressed to the contracting parties but which also contain obligations for private operators, such as shipmasters. we have identified two major contradictions in the commission's position. the first is that the proposal it has put forward with its own recommendations and management measures runs counter to the spirit of the convention, which aims to transpose those recommendations exactly, guaranteeing all fishermen and fleets in the region the same conditions. moreover, given that iccat recommendations enter into force six months after their adoption, it is incomprehensible that the 2008 recommendations, some of which are outdated, are only now being transposed, as is the case with swordfish fisheries in the mediterranean, for which new recommendations were drawn up last november. the delay is a sign of the limitations imposed by the fact that the member states have lost their capacity to manage marine resources for the european union, with inevitable damage to the management of resources at national level but also to the protection of arts which are of particular importance at regional level, because, being handmade, and we take as an example the jumping and pole in the azores or the almadraba in spain, they lack timely measures which ensure, in an uncompromising way, the protection of them and of all the historical, social and cultural heritage, in addition to the economic importance for the communities that maintain and practice them. we believe that the proposals as they have been put forward by iccat should be maintained, with management measures ensuring fishing opportunities, with particular attention to small-scale and artisanal fishing, with a view to the sustainability of tuna stocks. we also think that the commission's attempt to open the way to amendments and to centralise important decisions by means of delegated acts is a negative one. it is therefore considered that the direct transposition of the recommendations of the regional organisations is a more appropriate procedure, ensuring greater equity between the different countries.
mr president, respect for the sovereign and legitimate decision of the british people to leave the european union is not compatible with blackmail, threats or punishment. the deadlock and blockage of negotiations resulting from the contradictions of big british and european capital are not unrelated to an agenda that aims to pervert, circumvent and eventually reverse the decision of the british people. the neoliberal character of the forces involved, the nature of the ruling class on both sides of the negotiations, is evident in the announced intention to maintain and deepen common policies, particularly in the security, militaristic and refugee crisis response areas. in this context, it is essential to affirm the requirement that the rights of immigrants, including portuguese emigrants in the united kingdom, their labour rights and other social rights, such as residence, equal treatment or access to public services, the portability of social security benefits, family reunification or the mutual recognition of academic diplomas and professional qualifications, be ensured in the negotiations.
madam president, for reasons which are largely due to successive mistakes in the economic policy and the management of the refugee crisis in the european union, a majority of the voters in the united kingdom have decided to leave this union. the implementation of this legitimate decision must be carried out in full transparency. protecting the rights of european citizens and the future of the european project within the framework of the treaties is the top priority. portugal has a long tradition of bilateral cooperation with the united kingdom. this cooperation has always been possible within the framework of common membership of the european union. autonomy, identity and cooperation are pillars that do not cancel each other out but rather enhance each other. i am using my country as an example to make the message clearer that we must make clear in the european parliament's resolution. we want to continue to cooperate with the united kingdom. nothing moves us against your people. but we must do so within the framework of the integrity of the european institutions and their values and principles. with clear and transparent timetables. with guarantees of full respect for the rights of european citizens living in the united kingdom. ensuring the indivisibility and protecting the coherence and integrity of the european project. in short, we want the european project to remain strong and to learn from the experience of brexit so that the mistakes that led to it are not repeated.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, allow me to subscribe to and share the perplexity expressed by mrs liliana rodrigues, but the truth is that every year, every minute, 28 girls are forced to marry. that means 15 million a year, and if we do nothing, 150 million in the next decade. it is estimated that there are already 700 million women and 150 million men in the world who have been forced into marriage before they reach adulthood. numbers like these reinforce the sense of urgency in combating this reality, a fight which necessarily involves the union's external policy. countries such as nigeria, mozambique and india are examples where this reality still persists, but we need to act on our own territory too. it is urgent to ensure that we have the most appropriate means to eradicate this phenomenon from within our doors. it is even more intolerable that forced marriages exist within the european union. the european parliament recently approved the union's accession to the council of europe convention on preventing and combating violence against women and urged the member states not to do so, as anna maria corazza bildt recalled. in short, mr president, we must make this fight a priority, to rescue these children from forced marriages and to ensure that many more are not shackled in the future. a priority for everyone, in the member states and the rest of the world.

in writing. on 30 september the sugar quotas and the last agricultural quota system still in force in the european union came to an end. the decision to end sugar quotas now follows the 2013 reform of the common agricultural policy (cap) which started in 2006 and includes, among other wrongdoings, the end of milk quotas and vine planting rights. the end of sugar quotas has political significance, marking the end of a shift towards complete liberalization of supply, which is now governed solely by market law. but as reality shows, the market that the european union is promoting is nothing more than a mechanism designed to concentrate production in the richest countries by creating bonds of pure dependence and vassalage towards the poorest countries. with the agricultural crisis in the background, it is now imperative to stop this neoliberal drift at the level of the cap, either by replacing the instruments for regulating supply or at the level of the various free trade agreements which are putting unbearable pressure on our farmers.

on behalf of the gue/ngl group. mr president, the stability, coordination and governance of economic and monetary union was signed in 2012 in response to the 2008 financial crisis. but instead of learning lessons from the financial crisis, it has accentuated the most negative aspects of the stability and growth pact, thus wanting to kill the patient with the disease. as expected, the budget treaty only prolonged the divergence trajectory that had begun with the euro and the stability and growth pact, imposing austerity and the neoliberal agenda of the european union on the member states against the will of their peoples. as the situation in portugal shows, austerity is not inevitable, and by reversing the troika's measures, despite the european commission's blackmail and threats of sanctions and cuts in structural funds, wages and pensions have risen, taxes on labour have fallen and privatisations have been reversed, growth has returned and the deficit has even fallen. instead of incorporating the budgetary pact into the legal framework of the european union, it would be better to end the european economic union or at least allow the orderly withdrawal of those member states which so wish.
mr president, the single budgetary policy of the european union, with rules such as deficit limitation, restricts the ability of member states to respond to the economic cycle through their own budgetary policy. the budgetary treaty shows that the european union has learned nothing from history and what it does is to reinforce two perverse aspects of the rules that already exist, the ultra-liberal approach to budgetary rules, i.e. austerity as a rule, aggravating the problems of countries in responding to prolonged stagnation and high unemployment and by introducing the criterion of the structural balance, the arbitrariness that already existed in the imposition of the rules, treating countries unequally, which is now becoming a rule in itself. the integration of the budgetary treaty into community law will be tragic. the role of the socialists is decisive and i hope it will not be as tragic as it has been in previous moments. i call on all groups calling for the social state, protection of labour and a social europe to reject this conversion. i don't know if we'll make it in time to save the union. but not taking this step will be key to that.
mr president, the integration of the fiscal compact into the legal framework of the european union should be envisaged only if the lessons of recent years are properly taken into account. the first lesson is that this treaty is also about coordination and governance. when it comes to coordination, the objectives are not only stability but also growth and convergence. we have learned in recent years that this convergence should be guided by economic and social targets together and the european semester should be designed with this purpose. when it comes to reforms, the assumption is that the reforms which should be recommended to member states should take into account their real priorities and not invented priorities. but we also need to deal with the governance part of the treaty and the governance must be democratic, which is not yet the case. this means that, if a eurozone summit is now being convened, let me remind you that the treaty provides for parliament’s president to be invited. most of all, we need to make sure that, all through the process, parliament will play a central role: when we discuss the reforms of the economic and monetary union (emu), when we discuss the fiscal stance and when we discuss the recommendations for the euro area. we also need to recognise that there is a role for the national parliaments – yes, there is – when they are involved in discussing the national reform programmes and the country—specific recommendations. but overall we need an interinstitutional agreement to set the rules for real democratic governance of the emu. finally, on fiscal discipline, let me be clear: of course we need common fiscal discipline in the eurozone to make it sustainable, but it is also clear that, in order to have this common fiscal discipline, if we do not want to hinder growth and growth potential, we need to complement national fiscal discipline – which should be intelligent – with a proper fiscal capacity, as all other monetary zones in the world have. no monetary zone in the world has existed and been sustainable without a fiscal capacity. so these are our lessons to be integrated into the new framework.
madam president, the establishment of a european public prosecutor's office is a step forward in the process of european integration, in the com munisation and harmonisation of justice, particularly in the criminal field, with a view to a justice that serves the interests of the great powers and big business. the attack on the sovereignty of the states continues, reinforcing the federalist stance of the integration project, in this case the judiciary, through supranational bodies in which more and more competences and powers are concentrated, obliterating the necessary and desirable free and sovereign cooperation between national states. the creation of some of these european bodies and the strengthening of others are intended to protect and supersede the legitimate institutions of the member states, to restrict their ability to intervene on their own behalf in defence of their interests and to limit their judicial sovereignty. these proposals centralize, structure and strengthen bodies which escape democratic and judicial control, going beyond the guarantees of rights and freedoms provided for in national legislative and constitutional frameworks.
madam president, i too want to join the voices of my colleagues here who are calling for reform of interpol and for reform of red notices. there have been a number of cases where, for example, turkey has used this institute to harass and punish dissidents who leave turkish territory. and it's not just examples in spain, i'm from the czech republic, and we had a specific case like this in the czech republic. it may be known to the commission, it was the case of nazmi sahin, a turkish citizen of kurdish nationality, who was sentenced to six years in turkey for the offence of belonging to an organisation with political interests incompatible with the turkish state establishment. that means it was clearly a political crime. so there was a red notice, he left his homeland, there was a red notice from interpol, and the czech justice system dealt with this. the czech justice, of course, did not extradite this person, but the whole process took more than a year and the sahin in question was simply detained, he was restricted in his rights, he was in principle indirectly harassed, and the czech justice acted entirely in accordance with the law. i think that the reform must be carried out by interpol itself, the reform of red notices must be carried out, because the individual judicial authorities then decide how to proceed entirely according to the substantive law in the member state concerned. and even if the subject is not extradited, i think months of harassment is completely unnecessary. besides, what one of my speakers said is true, even if this person was not extradited by the czech republic, this red notice still applies and he can be detained in any other state and again the person concerned can get into trouble. that means he's basically permanently bullied. so i'm also calling for red notice reform.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the parliamentary assembly of the council of europe has adopted by a large majority resolution 2161, entitled 'abusive recourse to the interpol system: the need for more stringent legal safeguards'. interpol is an effective instrument of international cooperation in the fight against transnational crime, including terrorism, and is based on mutual assistance between national law enforcement authorities and should operate in complete neutrality and with respect for the human rights of suspects. the process of globalisation has opened up various channels of transnational criminal activity. the threats today are complex, numerous and destabilising, and international police and judicial cooperation is the answer to the internationalisation of crime. the international alert allows the police of the member states to share critical information concerning crimes that have occurred, in particular the red alert. available data tells us that over the past decade, the number of red alerts issued has increased dramatically. commissioner, i think that all this is possible as long as human rights and the rule of law are respected.
mr president, commissioner, although the european union has made great progress in road safety in recent decades, the high number of fatalities and serious injuries continues to cause great human suffering and unacceptable economic costs, estimated at eur 100 billion a year. in 2016, 25 000 people lost their lives on european union roads and another 135 000 were seriously injured. european commission data on road safety show a 2% decrease in the number of fatalities in 2016. however, while these figures are encouraging, they may not be enough for the european union to achieve its target of halving road deaths between 2010 and 2020. to stop these silent killers, the valletta declaration was signed in malta, which represents a common commitment by the member states of the european union to promote road safety and reduce accidents. one of the main objectives is to reduce by 50% the number of road fatalities by 2020, taking 2010 as a benchmark, and to reduce by 50% the number of serious injuries by 2030 compared to 2020. the 'europe on the move' package aims in particular to make traffic safer, recognising that action by all stakeholders is needed to reduce injuries and fatalities. the truth is, commissioner, that since the adoption of this package on 31 may, little progress has been made in road safety and other issues closely linked to road safety remain unanswered, issues linked - as the commissioner has mentioned here - to the fight against terrorism and the lack of coordination between the police in the fight against crime.
member of the commission. – mr president, i would like to thank honourable members for their report. although detention conditions are primarily the responsibility of member states, who have agreed to respect the existing council of europe standards on the matter, the commission attaches great importance to respect for the fundamental rights of persons in detention in the european union. improving prison conditions has become a priority at eu level because, among other reasons, they affect the efficient operation of eu mutual recognition instruments such as the european arrest warrant and because of the danger of radicalisation in prisons. the commission therefore fully supports this own—initiative report from the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs (libe). increasing prisons’ capacity is not the sole solution to overcrowding as the prison population tends to rise at the same rate as prison capacity. the focus should therefore rather be on the improvement of existing penitentiary structures, many of which in different member states are obsolete and do not function properly in terms of rehabilitation services. to facilitate that we have undertaken a mapping exercise to see how different eu funds could be employed to improve the situation in prisons in the eu. i have raised this several times with justice ministers, who are not always fully aware of the possibilities the european funds on regional and cohesion policy offer in that regard. but i would also like to mention the importance of alternatives to detention. member states have committed themselves under the eu framework decisions i just mentioned to accept alternative sentences, both in the pre—trial and the post-trial stage. even if the framework decisions oblige the member states to adopt a certain number of alternatives, for these alternatives to be used you need a certain practice and a developed probation structure. that is what is lacking in many member states. this is an area where a lot could be done at eu level. some member states are already well advanced in this area and others could learn from them. we could help to exchange best practices among member states. the last point i would like to touch upon is radicalisation. some of the individuals involved in terrorist attacks in europe were allegedly radicalised while serving prison sentences for other crimes. we need to find ways to prevent such violent radicalisation in our prisons and to help radicalised individuals to find other paths when they leave prison. following our ministerial conference on the matter in 2015 and the linked council conclusions, our priority is to use all available means, including financial, to support member states to speed up the setting up of projects such as the development of deradicalisation and rehabilitation programmes in and outside prisons, risk assessment tools and training schemes for prison staff, judges and prosecutors in the member states. to this effect we made eur 6.5 million available in 2015 and 2016 under the justice programme and continue to do so in 2017 and 2018. judicial training will remain an important factor. prison staff and probation officers are covered by the judicial training course. i would also like to remind you of the commission’s high—level expert group on radicalisation that will allow for further discussion with member states on the possible options for further and future more structured cooperation mechanisms in this area. one of the priority topics to be discussed is indeed radicalisation in prisons. i am planning to come back to this issue of radicalisation in prisons at a high-level meeting before the end of the year. the commission also pays particular attention to the children of parents in prison, of whom it is estimated that there are about 800 000 in the eu. therefore, this year’s european forum on the rights of the child, which will take place from 6 to 8 november 2017, will have a focus on the deprivation of liberty of children and alternatives to detention. furthermore, the situation of the children of parents in prison will also be addressed as one of the main topics. thank you again for your report as it supports our efforts in tackling the various aspects of detention in the eu.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, commissioner, first of all i would like to congratulate my colleague, joëlle bergeron, on her excellent work. thank you very much. in 2014, there were more than half a million prisoners in prisons in the european union, including those sentenced to a definitive sentence and those who have been charged with a crime and are in preventive detention, which, according to eurostat data, is more than 20% of the total prison population. while the conditions of detention and the management of prisons are the responsibility of the member states, the union also has a necessary role to play in safeguarding the fundamental rights of prisoners and in creating the european area of freedom, security and justice. there are considerable differences between the judicial and prison systems used in the countries of the union, in particular as regards the adoption of alternative measures, detention and sentences substituting for short-term sentences. in 1999 this house adopted an important resolution on prison conditions in the union, adaptations and substitution sentences. we are discussing this again after almost 20 years, and many of the assumptions and situations identified at that time are the same as those we are discussing here today. i believe that there is a need for convergence of the forms of the exercise of justice in the member states, despite the difficulties this implies. today, as the commissioner said, we are witnessing serious problems in the prison system, with prison overcrowding, the use of illicit substances, an alarming rate of suicides and radicalisation in many of the union's prisons. these are very worrying phenomena which require particular attention. for all these reasons, ladies and gentlemen, i believe that the institutions of the union should support, as far as possible, the technical and economic improvement of prison systems and conditions, especially in those member states which are facing serious financial difficulties. the situation in prisons in europe is worrying and the european parliament has an important role to play in reversing this situation.
prisons are designed to educate offenders so that they can return to social life free of criminal tendencies. unfortunately, in many countries of the european union, prisons have become real schools of crime. many inmates who have been convicted of occasional offenses leave prison transformed into professional criminals. on the other hand, criminals are sentenced by the courts to a penalty which deprives them of their freedom but does not deprive them of their dignity as human beings. it has been shown that the member states alone do not have the capacity to change this state of affairs. only the creation, commissioner, of a european prison model can bring about a positive change in this situation. this european penitentiary model, the only one in europe, is also a requirement of european integration.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, more than a year ago we adopted a resolution here on the violations of the human rights of people with albinism in africa, particularly in malawi. since then we have seen an alarming resurgence of murders and attacks against albinos in east africa, in malawi, tanzania, south africa and mozambique. albinos, including many children, continue to pay a terrible price for their condition. in addition to being rejected by their own families, they are victims of gangs of criminals and traffickers, often protected by the police, who live off the harvesting of their body parts to feed the macabre and very lucrative business of magic potions associated with the belief that they possess magical powers. this house cannot but strongly condemn the violence and discrimination against people with albinism and urge the authorities in these countries to put an end to the impunity of those responsible and to support all campaigns which, by raising awareness of the causes of this congenital disorder, make it possible to combat discrimination, prejudice and superstition. we cannot tolerate the continuation of this genuine genetic apartheid.

mr president, commissioner, any intervention in the field of the marketing of fertilising products must not lose sight of the fact that the dominant european agriculture is moving away from fundamental aspects of a sustainable production model, which will not be alien to the policies and guidelines prevailing in the european union, such as the cap, which need a profound modification. the rapporteur believes that member states should be free to allow non-harmonised fertilisers on the internal market, without depriving economic operators of the opportunity to benefit from a harmonised regulatory framework. if, on the one hand, we think it wise to restrict the effects of harmonization by making it optional, on the other hand, the criteria which have guided the elaboration of the harmonized regulatory framework seem to us more questionable. these criteria would be expected to ensure high standards of quality, safety and protection of both the environment and public health, to stimulate or at least not to compromise product and process innovation, to be in line with the best available technical and scientific knowledge and not to benefit certain types of economically powerful interests and operators to the detriment of others. there are legitimate doubts that this is so.
in 2016, the commission adopted a proposal laying down rules on the making available on the market of ce marked fertilising products, covering a wider range of such products and opening the single market to the free movement of fertilising products produced from organic or secondary raw materials. while it is true that fertilisers provide significant benefits in terms of crop yield, it is equally true that we cannot ignore the significant environmental, public health and food safety challenges associated with the use of fertilisers. the main objective of the report is to encourage the large-scale production of fertilisers in the union from organic or national secondary raw materials in accordance with the circular economy model by converting waste into nutrients for crops. however, the current regulation does not address environmental issues associated with contamination of soil, inland water and marine waters and, ultimately, foodstuffs. and cadmium is a sad example of that. ladies and gentlemen, i therefore believe that the maximum levels of cadmium should be as low as possible in order to ensure a high level of protection for human health. and this is what i have to say at this moment.
the exchange of information between member states, the existence and operation of a rapid alert system and the risk assessment procedures for new psychoactive substances are of great importance. the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction will currently monitor more than 450 substances. in recent years, there has been an unprecedented increase in the number, type and availability of psychoactive substances, which coincides with the persistence of a deep and protracted social crisis, with very significant levels of unemployment, poverty and social exclusion, with the continued attack on social and labour rights, with the weakening of public services. to address this complex reality, a comprehensive approach is recommended, integrating the strands of prevention, deterrence, risk reduction and harm minimisation, through to treatment and social reintegration. there is a need to develop a better understanding of a rapidly changing reality, both in terms of substances and consumption patterns and their prevalence in different population groups. it is necessary to identify constraints in access to the healthcare delivery system and to expand, diversify and adequately staff the public network of services in the area of drug dependence, with a focus on a local response. this path requires confronting and reversing the effects of the policies of cutting public spending that the european union has been imposing on the member states.
mr president, to speak of minimum income policies is to speak of the real causes which justify them; the commission's proposal is to discuss the reasons why 125 million people in the european union, which produces 20% of world gdp, are living in poverty today; it is to discuss the reasons why economic and social inequalities, levels of labour exploitation or precariousness are increasing. the answer is clear: it is in the capitalist matrix of the european union and the policies it has promoted that the causes justifying the need for these instruments lie - transitional instruments which are necessary but which do not address the underlying causes. these are fought with real ruptures, with the constraints that eu policies impose on states and prevent policies of effective redistribution of wealth, the promotion of decent work with rights, the advances of collective agreements, real wage increases, the fight against inequalities or the guarantee of free and quality public services, health, social security and education finally, progressive development policies that respond to the needs of workers and peoples.
mr president, the introduction and strengthening of minimum income policies, if properly combined with other measures and policies relating to the social and labour market, can be a way of combating poverty and promoting social inclusion and access to the labour market. i also believe that minimum income schemes can also act as automatic macroeconomic stabilisers in response to economic shocks. in 2015, 23,7% of the population and 12,5% of workers aged over 18 in the european union were at risk of poverty and social exclusion. ladies and gentlemen, europe is one of the most prosperous regions in the world, but these figures show a harsh reality that must be tackled. the european social observatory has been reporting that 26 member states already have income support schemes, but there are still many differences between member states in the treatment of minimum income policies. i therefore agree with this report, which calls in particular on all member states to introduce adequate minimum income schemes as a last line of social protection, as a way of fighting poverty and ensuring a decent existence for all those who lack sufficient resources, and proposes that the commission consider the creation of an appropriate european fund, on a temporary basis, to act as an instrument of european cohesion and society policy.

mr president, commissioner, mr president of the council, ladies and gentlemen, once again we have a united parliament with clear priorities: we want more employment, especially for young people, we want external and internal solidarity, we want a competitive economy and we want social and territorial cohesion. it is also a repeat of the council's cuts, again without any rational basis. the council is cutting into its own priorities - cuts against its own proclamations and cuts against its own commitments. it is not acceptable, for example, to cut horizon 2020, which is an excellent implementation, which is the largest research programme in the world and which is essential for innovation and for the competitiveness of our economy. i challenge you to explain why you are cutting 491 million euros in commitments and 111 million euros in payments in this programme. the youth employment initiative must be improved, but it must continue. support for the backbone of our economy, which is smes, must also be increased. cohesion policy is essential. external solidarity is also important. and then, commissioner and council, it is essential that we increase the implementation of cohesion policy. it is also essential that we have sufficient payments for the purpose of these programmes, which are in the hands and management of the member states. the budget of the european union is only 1% of gross national income. it would be interesting to ask how much it would cost the european union not to have this budget. i have no doubt that it would cost much more than 1% of gross national income, which proves the need for an increase, but also the importance and added value of this budget.
mr president, this budget is constrained by a financial framework that is short of the objectives proclaimed by the institutions of the european union and reflects a profound crisis of confidence which has led the member states to cut back on this multiannual plan. a financial framework which cuts back on cohesion and the common agricultural policy in favour of the defense and armaments headings, and which this budget naturally expresses, as in the case of the external action service, which is seeking to recruit five thousand people. a budget allocating to turkey twice as much as is provided for the erasmus programme, on the basis of a deal concluded by germany against the wishes of the other member states, but to which no one objected. a budget that focuses on financial instruments and the conditionality of support, while cutting back on research funds. in short, this is a budget which does not present any consistent solutions to boost investment and to counteract the path of social and economic divergence which is being accentuated by the deepening of this process of capitalist integration of the european union.
on behalf of the alde group. madam commissioner, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i welcome this report and the rapporteur on the protection of workers at risk from exposure to carcinogens at work, firstly because it expressly endorses the precautionary principle, which can be summed up roughly as follows: in the event of doubt as to the effects of certain substances on human health, workers should be prevented from being exposed to these substances, even if this means their removal from the workplace. the commission has also undertaken to carry out a study by the end of the first quarter of 2019 on the inclusion of re-prothotics in a future review of the directive. this review is justified by concerns that reprotoxins may pose risks to reproductive health, in particular to the development of the foetus, as well as during the breastfeeding period. there is, however, a concern, commissioner, mr president, which, although not specifically raised by the content of this report, is always present in political debates involving scientific questions. i am referring to the frequency with which scientific opinions on the same subjects are diametrically opposed. how can there be scientists who attest that certain substances are not harmful to human health, while others guarantee precisely the opposite? it is necessary to denounce and combat those who use science for the spurious economic interests of certain companies and entrepreneurs. in the age of fake news, we also need to fight these fake reports and these lies under the guise of science. thank you very much.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, agustín díaz de mera, for the excellent work he has done, whose seriousness and competence we have long admired. the entry/exit system also aims to contribute to the collective effort to make our borders safer. for that reason, the system is welcome. however, i have said many times and from the beginning that i have doubts about the added value of this system. it is intended, in particular, to detect overstayers, i.e. third-country nationals who overstay their visas. this information will be mainly quantitative, but will not allow you to locate them. i doubt that its high cost is justified by its superior value. i have doubts about its implementation, i have doubts about its real added value, especially in the light of the information systems already in place and in the field, such as the sis (the schengen information system). we must look at the management of our borders in an integrated way. information systems must be at the service of this strategy. but let 's be clear: the court of justice's judgment on the pnr agreement between the union and canada. i am confident in the soundness of the negotiated text and i will end as i began: i have no doubt about the work carried out by the rapporteur on behalf of this parliament.
mr president, the roma are part of europe's culture and values, but we all know that there is latent hostility towards the roma at all levels of european society and in all geographical areas of europe. i therefore voted in favour of this resolution in recognition of the fact that roma must be considered vulnerable people and that the charter of fundamental rights of the european union prohibits any form of discrimination. the special case of women and girls who are victims of discrimination, which feeds a vicious circle of inequality, exclusion and marginalisation, must be carefully monitored and combated. in 2016, only 16% of ethnic roma women were employed, 72% of roma girls were not in education or employment, and 71% of roma women aged 18-24 dropped out of education or training early. ladies and gentlemen, this resolution which we are adopting today expresses deep concern about discrimination against roma and proposes relevant measures to ensure that roma have access to health care, social services, education, justice, employment opportunities and housing. i believe that the implementation of these actions is undoubtedly an expression of a fair, humanistic and inclusive europe.
mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr vice-president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen, the fight against illegal immigration and human trafficking must be a priority. and not just in the mediterranean. but the great challenge in the mediterranean is the flow of refugees fleeing war and whom we have a duty to protect. unfortunately, tens of thousands of people have died in recent years seeking refuge on our continent. the real debate, the honest discussion on the mediterranean, must focus on responding to the humanitarian crisis, on fighting human trafficking and on creating legal access routes. that's why, as early as 2013, we mandated that search and rescue be a priority in frontex operations. that is why we have given this agency and europol greater capacity to combat trafficking. and that is also why this parliament recently adopted its position on a mechanism for the resettlement of refugees from third countries and on the new dublin regulation. parliament was right to insist on a holistic approach and not a single sectoral approach with the report by mr metsola and mr kyenge. saving lives, legal access and fighting crime must all be our priorities without exception.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, commissioner, it is very satisfactory that the unemployment rate and the youth unemployment rate in the european union have fallen to around 9.5% and 19.4% respectively. but we can't put our arms down. despite improvements, there are still 14 million young europeans without work, education or training. this is terrible and unacceptable. in addition to the tremendous social impact, it has a cost of €153 billion for the european union, knowing that the wider implementation of the youth guarantee throughout the union would cost us much less. we need to be more incisive in its implementation, strengthening it at national, regional and local level, aware of the importance of this guarantee in preparing young people for the labour market. on the other hand, it is also urgent to strengthen their monitoring in order to avoid mechanisms that falsify employment figures by means of traineeships and other training programmes, creating the illusion of a recovery that is in fact too fragile, without guarantees of sustainability. we have to be prepared for the future. we must be demanding in our commitment to education and training, especially in developing digital skills, to face the new challenges. this is an essential condition for defending the european social model, which requires the sustainability of our social security systems. it is precisely in defending this european social model, in balance with economic sustainability, that the european semester should be based. in this connection, we have proposed a procedure for social imbalances which, although not punitive, aims to strengthen the social dimension of european policies, so as to create a genuine european social pillar that will guarantee the future of the union, that will guarantee the future of our young people.
on behalf of the s&d group, i would like to say that this is an important moment for the democratic governance of economic and monetary union. in fact, in the report we are discussing today, the european parliament, elected directly by the citizens, has spoken on the direction of the economic policy of the euro zone. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, gunnar hökmark, because despite our political differences - which are obviously many - we have once again had the opportunity to continue our constructive work towards a balanced and overall positive report. i believe that is what we have before us today: a balanced and overall positive report. it is important to start by recognising the signs of economic recovery that we have been seeing in the euro area. we now have more growth, less unemployment, less deficit and more controlled public debt. but the first message from this parliament is a warning message. the european economic recovery we have is still too weak, too fragile and too uneven. despite the juncker plan and the turnaround of austerity in some countries, this recovery of the economy is still too much supported by an expansionary monetary policy of the european central bank which cannot last forever. that is why the second message of this report and of this parliament is that we urgently need to overcome the investment deficit that still exists in the european economy. there are certainly many structural changes to correct the architecture of the euro area and to defend our economies from speculation in sovereign debt markets, but the priority for investment is inescapable. we need a reinforced juncker plan with a fairer geographical distribution, but we also need a different budgetary policy, making full use of the flexibility clauses, and this is a central issue. the de facto finding of this report is that while the european commission has proposed a moderately expansionary budgetary policy, we will continue to have a budgetary policy which is essentially neutral. this is what we need to correct in order to restore the european economy's ambition for convergence and the ambition for economic growth and investment.
mr president, securitisation is at the root of the process of financialisation of the economy and at the heart of the speculative bubble that burst in 2008 and plunged europe and the world into a social and economic crisis, the impact of which still remains in our economies. securitisation is an instrument created by bankers to clean up their balance sheets, pooling more or less dubious credits and creating financial products that have never been, nor will be, simple and much less transparent. this relaunch of the securitisation market represents a step in the wrong direction. europe does not need more finance. europe needs more real economy, more jobs and more development. to speak of a lack of liquidity when the ecb has been flooding europe with asset purchases for months is at least strange. in short, what we need is a public financial system that serves the economy of businesses and workers. but this objective is not the aim of this neoliberal european union, which is at the service of finance and large multinational companies.
mr president, australia and new zealand are among the few countries in the world with which the union does not have, or is not negotiating, a trade or investment agreement. we now have the opportunity to change this absurd situation by launching these negotiations, which are already late. australia and new zealand are important allies and partners of the union, sharing the same values as us, including the ambition for a high level of social and environmental protection and a regulated global trading system. we therefore have all the conditions to conclude progressive, new-generation agreements which promote sustainable development, and it is for the european parliament to ensure transparency but also to safeguard european interests, including in sensitive areas which obviously need to be negotiated, such as geographical indications, the meat, milk, cereals and sugar sectors. if we do that, if we make progressive and balanced agreements, then we will be living up to the demands of the european citizens.
